| DRF-K, DRF-X, DRF-H (7.2 to 7.4) | T1 | KILLED(T1b as specified: a narrow, cold-started second pass; the kills rest on one clean size) | — | — | Logged |
| 6.7, 6.7F | T0, T1 | KILLED (accepted) | — | — | Logged |

* **Live routes: 3.** Routes 8.3 and 8.2 have passed the premise, and 8.4 is GATED. No route is at T2, so nothing is submitted.
#### Local Autopsy Log (critic additions)
* **Route 8.1**: killed at T0. It is the Lanczos-step sign of route 6.8 at initialization, made cheap by locality, plus trainable flips that SR leaves unused.
* **Restated anti-pattern (replaces "frozen-frame ratios are not a cheap stand-in for exact ones")**: *a fixed thresholded vote on frozen ratios is harmful on a trained backflow, but the same ratios used as learned features are informative*. Frozen and exact ratio features locate the trained foil's residual wrong signs about equally well ($\mathrm{dAUC}$ 0.28 against 0.31 at $V=12$).
* **Restated anti-pattern (replaces the causal clause of "an information test on the starting determinant does not predict a trained gain")**: the anti-pattern stands, but its cause is not wrong-signed features. On the trained base the information is present. What remains unexplained is the conversion step, which T1b tested only with a narrow, cold-started second pass.
* **New process anti-pattern**: *a counted toy size must have a gap*. The 5×2 4+4 ladder has $E_1-E_0=0.0041t$, so wrong-sign labels there can follow the near-degenerate partner (weight 0.45 for one foil seed). Every T1 in this leaf asserts $E_1-E_0\ge0.02t$.

### Round 8 — Trained T1 of routes 8.3 and 8.4 (2026-11-16)
Scope: the critic's pre-registered T1 for 8.3 (DRF-WS), then 8.4 (DRF-T) after 8.3 was killed. Both ran against the full-width NNBF foil on gap-asserted sizes. New code in `tests/P1.2_unanchored_antisymmetry_r6/`: `t1c_warm.py` (the foil stage, the warm-started arms, checks, budget ledger and verdict). Outputs are `t1c_*_output.txt` and `t1c_train_*.txt`, results are in `results/t1c/`, and the CPU ledger is `results/t1c/ledger.json`. Job 553724 was not touched. No job was submitted.
#### Design (docstring of `t1c_warm.py`, written before any run)
* **Sizes** (the critic's list), each with the gap assert $E_1-E_0\ge0.02t$ (`t1c_gaps_output.txt`):
    * size 0 is 4×2 3+3 ($V=8$, gap 0.066);
    * size 1 is 6×2 5+5 ($V=12$, gap 0.118);
    * size 2 is 4×3 5+5 ($V=12$, gap 0.085).
* **Largest size.** Sizes 1 and 2 have the same $V$ and $D$. "The largest" in the rule is size 2, the last in the critic's list and the only one with a 3-leg direction. This was fixed before any run.
* **Start.** The foil `nnbfW` (width 6, UHF start, 300 SR steps, seeds 11 and 22) is trained once, saved, and loaded by every arm. At size 1 it reproduces the T1b `2_nnbfW` values (0.01280 and 0.00934).
* **Arms**, each trained for 300 more SR steps from the saved foil:
    * `ws` (8.3) is the DRF with frame H, pass 1 the trained foil, and pass 2 starting at $W_4=0$. "The largest $H_2$ that fits" is fixed as the largest $H_2$ with $n_{\mathrm{params}}\le2T$. That gives $H_2=6=H_1$ at $V=12$ and 2196 parameters, against $H_2=2$ in T1b (critic Major 2).
    * `wide` is the foil widened to width 14 (2270 parameters). Its new hidden units have zero output rows, so it starts from the foil's state.
    * `dt` (8.4) is the tied re-entry, which is DRF with $H_2=H_1$ and $W_4$ tied to $W_2$, starting at $W_3=b_3=0$ (1476 parameters).
    * `widet` is the foil widened to width 9 (1545 parameters).
* **Rule.** This is the critic's rule, with $r=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\text{route}]/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\text{its wide foil}]$ as a median over two seeds.
* **Checks** (`t1c_checks_output.txt`, `t1c_checks2_output.txt`; all pass):
    * CHECK A: every warm start reproduces the foil's amplitudes exactly (relative difference 0).
    * CHECK B: the force of `dt` matches finite differences to $3.4\times10^{-8}$ (feature G) and $8.7\times10^{-7}$ (feature O). Its per-sample log-derivatives match the force rows to $2\times10^{-16}$.
    * CHECK D: the faster forward pass (full $G=O_1A^{-1}$ followed by one lookup, plus reuse of the amplitude pass in the force) matches the T1b `DRF` to $6\times10^{-15}$ in amplitudes and force.
* **Budget.** RLIMIT_CPU is 540 s per invocation. Three SR steps per arm are timed before the heavy part, and a task ledger aborts any invocation whose projection would take the task over 1800 CPU-s. One invocation was aborted by this gate and split. Actual use was **1687 CPU-s (28.1 CPU-minutes)**, including 30 s of hand-logged diagnostics. The largest invocation used 343 CPU-s, and every invocation stayed under 10 CPU-minutes.
#### Route 8.3 DRF-WS: KILLED at T1

```
  8.3 size 0: dEs foil 0.00401 wide 0.00075 ws 0.00066 r 0.873 counted False
  8.3 size 1: dEs foil 0.01107 wide 0.00866 ws 0.00858 r 0.991 counted True
  8.3 size 2: dEs foil 0.04962 wide 0.03801 ws 0.03766 r 0.991 counted True
VERDICT route 8.3: KILL
```

* **Per seed** (seeds 11 and 22): at size 1, `ws` has 0.00868 and 0.00848, and `wide` has 0.00811 and 0.00920. At size 2, `ws` has 0.03596 and 0.03936, and `wide` has 0.03732 and 0.03870. The seed ranges overlap at both sizes, so this is a tie, not a loss.
* **Reading.**
    * The warm start removes both T1b confounds. The second pass trains normally: $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ falls from 0.073 to 0.052 at size 1, below `wide` at 0.065, and from 0.162 to 0.124 at size 2.
    * But its sign error ties width ($r=0.99$ at both counted sizes). The gain of the second pass goes into amplitudes, not signs.
    * This happens although its input features locate the foil's wrong signs with held-out AUC 0.93 (critic premise).
    * The conversion step therefore fails when it is given a converged base, a pass as wide as pass 1, and gapped sizes.
#### Route 8.4 DRF-T: T0 kept, T1 UNDECIDED
* **T0 after the 8.3 kill.** The sharpest gap ("re-entry through the shared $W_2$ moves the orbitals only along directions the foil already learned") was not settled by 8.3, because 8.4 changes the parameter allocation and 8.3 does not. Gates 0 to 4 stand as the critic wrote them, so 8.4 went to T1.
* **As run, the arm did not train.** At $V=12$ the `dt` arm never left its warm start. The first SR step of the recipe (shift $10^{-3}$, lr 0.05) is clipped to norm 1 and raises $E$ by 0.68. The best-energy guard of `p21_harness.optimize` then returns the start, so `dt` at size 1 equals the foil to every printed digit.
* **Probes** (8 steps from the size-1 seed-11 start): lr 0.01 and 0.003 also diverge, a shift of $10^{-2}$ diverges, and a shift of $10^{-1}$ descends monotonically.
* **Amendment, written in the docstring before any `dts` run.** Arm `dts` is `dt` trained with an SR shift of 0.1, the only change. `widet` keeps the recipe, because it trained normally. This change can only favour the route, so a KILL of `dts` would be final. A PASS-W would also need `widet` retrained at shift 0.1. The `dt` rows decide nothing. Size 0 of `dts` was not run, to stay inside the task cap; it is uncounted (foil median 0.004) and decides nothing.

```
  8.4-as-run size 1: dEs foil 0.01107 widet 0.00821 dt 0.01107 r 1.348 counted True
  8.4 size 1: dEs foil 0.01107 widet 0.00821 dts 0.00979 r 1.191 counted True
  8.4 size 2: dEs foil 0.04962 widet 0.04450 dts 0.03985 r 0.895 counted True
VERDICT route 8.4: UNDECIDED
```

* **Per seed.** At size 1, `dts` has 0.01053 and 0.00905, and `widet` has 0.00744 and 0.00899, so `dts` loses for both seeds. At size 2, `dts` has 0.03917 and 0.04052, and `widet` has 0.04622 and 0.04278, so `dts` wins for both seeds.
* **Reading.**
    * At size 2, $r=0.895$ misses the KILL threshold of 0.9 by 0.005. That decides the verdict, since KILL needs $r\ge0.9$ at the largest size. No reading is near PASS-W ($r\le0.7$ everywhere).
    * The size-2 win is not attributable. `dts` and `widet` differ in optimizer shift as well as in architecture, and `widet` barely improved on the foil in energy at size 2 ($\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ from 0.162 to 0.150, against 0.114 for `dts`).
    * The wider foil of 8.3 (`wide`, width 14, 0.0380) beats `dts` (0.0399) at size 2.
* **Pre-registered tie-break (written now; not run, because the task cap leaves 113 CPU-s).** Retrain `widet` with SR shift 0.1 at sizes 1 and 2 (arm `widets`, about 140 CPU-s) and apply the same rule with $r=\mathrm{dts}/\mathrm{widets}$. KILL closes 8.4. PASS-W needs the `dtsO` attribution run. UNDECIDED again kills 8.4 at T1 for lack of a size trend, with no further seeds.
#### Route 8.2 DRF-E: KILLED at T0 (inheritance clause)
* **Inheritance.** The critic wrote this clause before 8.3 ran: "If 8.3 fails because a second pass cannot convert information that is present, 8.2 inherits that failure." 8.3 failed in exactly that way. The second pass trained, its frozen features carried AUC 0.93 of wrong-sign information, and the sign error tied width ($r=0.99$).
* **Size of the exact-ratio advantage.** Exact features add only $\Delta\mathrm{dAUC}=0.03$ over frozen ones at $V=12$ (AUC 0.96 against 0.93). For 8.2 to reach $r\le0.7$, that small increment would have to produce a 30% sign gain where the larger frozen information produced none.
* **Budget.** The T1 would need new code (a local pass 1, low-rank exact ratios and their gradient), and the task's T1 budget is spent.
* **Status.** The T0 kill is flagged for the per-leaf critic.

#### Local Autopsy Log (Round 8 additions)
* **Route 8.3**: killed at T1. A warm-started second pass, as wide as the first and fed frozen ratios that locate the foil's wrong signs with AUC 0.93, lowers the total energy faster than width but leaves the sign error equal to width's ($r=0.99$ at 6×2 and 4×3).
* **New anti-pattern (for the master doc)**: *located sign errors are not corrected sign errors*. Even when a feature locates where a trained backflow's sign is wrong (AUC 0.93), SR on a ratio-fed orbital update spends the new capacity on amplitudes. The sign error moves only as much as width moves it.
* **New process anti-pattern**: *an exact-identity warm start through a shared, trained output layer can be SR-unstable*. With the tied re-entry and $W_3=0$, the first SR step at shift $10^{-3}$ is clipped and raises $E$ by 0.68, and the best-energy guard silently returns the start. Every warm-started arm must log that it left its start (check $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ against the start) before its row is scored.

### Closed-leaf falsifier (2026-11-16)
Scope: the idle-time queue item "falsifiers of closed leaves" (SKILL.md Stage 3.5.3 step 3), plus the cheapest projected-control re-check asked for by the cross-node critic (Majors 3 and 4: unprojected toys and a 300-step learning-speed statement). Code: `tests/P1.2_unanchored_antisymmetry_r6/t1e_closed.py`. Both rules are in its docstring and were written before any run. Outputs: `t1e_checks_output.txt`, the `t1e_trainA_size*.txt` and `t1e_foilP_size*.txt` logs, and `t1e_verdict_output.txt`; results are in `results/t1e/`. No job was submitted and no running job was touched.
#### Part A: the Saturation Diagnostic's own falsifier (route LRK)
* **Carrier.** LRK is the diagnostic's "minimal extra structure", built as the diagnostic describes. Pass 1 is the trained foil. Its frame-H ratio features (the DRF features, which locate the foil's wrong signs with AUC 0.93) feed a weight-shared per-site kernel. The kernel input is $\phi_v=(x_{\uparrow v},x_{\downarrow v},f_{0v},f_{1v},f_{2v})$, with $K=8$ hidden units. It outputs $U_s(\mathbf n)$ and $T_s(\mathbf n)$, both $V\times R$ with $R=2$, and the orbitals become $O_{2,s}=O_{1,s}+U_s\,(T_s^{\mathsf T}O_{1,s})$. Every orbital thus gains configuration-dependent site directions.
    * The carrier has 120 parameters at every $V$, below one width unit (145 at $V=12$).
    * Its warm start is exact ($U=0$).
    * The foil `wid` is the trained foil widened to 1255 parameters, so $P/D=0.075$ with $D=16632$ (unprojected).
    * Both arms use the same warm start, 300 more SR steps, the t1c recipe, and seeds 11 and 22.
* **Checks** (all pass). Every warm start reproduces the foil exactly. The LRK force matches finite differences to $3.4\times10^{-7}$ (feature G) and $3.3\times10^{-8}$ (feature O). The per-sample log-derivatives match the force rows to $3\times10^{-16}$.
* **Rule A** is the diagnostic's falsifier under t1c's rule, with $r=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\mathrm{lrk}]/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\mathrm{wid}]$. Only a PASS-W at the counted sizes plus an attribution PASS falsifies the diagnostic.

| size | foil $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ | `wid` | `lrk` | $r$ | counted |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 0 (4×2, 3+3) | 0.00401 | 0.00168 | 0.00204 | 1.21 | no |
| 1 (6×2, 5+5) | 0.01107 | 0.00850 | 0.00892 | 1.05 | yes |
| 2 (4×3, 5+5) | 0.04962 | 0.04267 | 0.04269 | 1.00 | yes |

* **Verdict A: KILL. The diagnostic's falsifier did not fire, and the diagnostic stands.** LRK lowers the total energy more than width does (at size 2, $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ 0.120 and 0.123 against 0.134 and 0.137), but it leaves the sign error unchanged. That is the same pattern as 8.3: the ratio-fed update goes to amplitudes, not nodes. The attribution control `lrkO` was not run, because the rule calls for it only after a PASS-W.
#### Part B: projected-control re-check of the 8.3 kill
* **Design.** The space-group projection $P_\chi$ has $\lvert G\rvert=4$; ED puts $\chi$ in the fully symmetric irrep of $M_x$ and $M_y$. SR runs on $\psi_P$. The projected per-sample log-derivatives match $\mathrm{force}(P_\chi e_n)/\psi_P(n)$ to $2\times10^{-15}$ for LRK, DRF and NNBF. The projected cold foil is NNBF width 6 after 300 projected SR steps. It is followed by `wsP` (8.3) against `wideP` (width 14) for 600 steps. The comparison counts only if the projected foil's median $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}\ge0.005$, tried at size 2 first and then at size 1.
* **Result: NO-TARGET-P at both toys.** The projected foil's median $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ is 0.00448 at 4×3 (seeds 0.00410 and 0.00486) and 0.00166 at 6×2 (0.00111 and 0.00221). The unprojected foils had 0.0496 and 0.0111. So the 8.3 comparison cannot be re-run under projection on these toys, and `wsP` and `wideP` were not trained.
* **Reading.**
    * About 90% of the unprojected sign error on which the closing T1s were scored is symmetry contamination, which projection removes. This confirms the round-2 anti-pattern quantitatively for this leaf.
    * The closing kills compare two unprojected arms, so their ratios stay fair (cross-node critic Major 4). But "no mechanism converts location faster than width" is a statement about **unprojected** $V\le12$ toys at 300 steps only.
    * On projected toys of ED size, NNBF width 6 already leaves less than 0.005 of sign error, so no projected toy of this family can test the diagnostic. The projected evidence on the target instance remains P1.3 and the 553724 T2.
* **Budget.** The leaf ledger is 866 CPU-s (14.4 CPU-minutes) out of 1800. The largest invocation used 318 CPU-s, with one thread throughout. The budget gate aborted one invocation before any heavy work (projected 667 CPU-s). It was rerun after the LRK forward pass was switched to the FastDRF Green's-function gather, which halved the step cost.
* **Leaf status: stays CLOSED.** The diagnostic's falsifier was run and killed, and the projected re-check is NO-TARGET-P. Neither reopens the leaf. A scope note was added to the status line.

* **Job 553724 cancelled (2026-11-16).** Its pre-registered verdict (FAIL) was already decided from the stored seeds and the leaf is closed, so finishing it could not teach anything new. Five tasks had completed, three were cancelled.

### Reopening review (2026-11-16)
Scope: the user's question "is each subproblem unsolvable, or already solved?" A closed leaf is neither. This review turns the Saturation Diagnostic's named ingredient and untried regimes into new routes, gates them, and tests the survivors at T1 only. Code is in `tests/P1.2_unanchored_antisymmetry_r9/`: `t1f_phase.py` (route RPC), `t1f_curv.py` (the phase-family premise) and `t1g_projnarrow.py` (route LRK-PN). Each rule is in its docstring and was written before the run it governs. Outputs are the `t1f_*` and `t1g_*` text files, results are in `results/t1f/` and `results/t1g/`, and the CPU ledger is `results/t1f/ledger.json`. No job was submitted, no job was cancelled, and no earlier file was edited.
#### 1. What the diagnostic names
* **Named ingredients.**
    * I1: a sign carrier that is continuous in its parameters, is fed the ratio information, and adds new orbital directions for less than one width unit. LRK was built to this description and tied width ($r$ 1.05 and 1.00).
    * I2: an attribution control showing that a gain needs the inverse. It has never run, because no arm reached PASS-W.
    * I3 (implicit in "located sign errors are not corrected sign errors"): a carrier whose new capacity goes to the nodes rather than to the amplitudes.
* **Untried regimes.**
    * U1: projected training with a target. Width 6 leaves less than 0.005 of projected sign error (NO-TARGET-P), so the diagnostic was scoped to unprojected toys.
    * U2: horizons past 300 steps, and comparisons at equal wall time rather than equal parameters (cross-node critic Major 3).
    * U3: a carrier that changes the sign without passing through zero. Barrier (b) is cited as the mechanism, but the only carriers tried without a zero crossing were Route 4 (fitted to oracle labels) and the complex detour 6.4 (two independent parts).
    * U4: exact ratios. Route 8.2 was killed by inheritance and never run.
    * U5: sizes above $V=12$, and the $4\times4$ target instance.
#### 2. New candidate routes and gates
* **Route 9.1, RPC: ratio-fed phase carrier (Operation B.1, real frame to phase frame; built from I3 and U3).**
    * *Ansatz.* $\psi=\psi_F(\mathbf n)\,e^{i\varphi(\mathbf n)}$, where $\psi_F$ is the trained foil, held frozen. The phase is $\varphi(\mathbf n)=\pi\sum_v[\tanh(\phi_vW_k+b_k)\cdot a+c]$, where $\phi_v$ is LRK's per-site input (occupations plus frame-H frozen ratio features). The kernel is weight-shared, with $K=8$ hidden units and 57 parameters at every $V$. The sign is read out as $s=\mathrm{sgn}(\psi_F\cos\varphi)$.
    * *Why it addresses I3 and U3.* A sign changes by rotation, so $\lvert\psi\rvert$ never passes through zero. For real symmetric $H$, $E[\varphi]=\sum_{nm}\psi_n\psi_mH_{nm}\cos(\varphi_n-\varphi_m)/N$, where $N=\sum_n\psi_n^2$. The real and imaginary parts share one amplitude, so the energy couples them. In the killed route 6.4 they were independent networks.
    * Gate 0: PASS, narrowly. A WebSearch on 2026-11-16 found a neural sign-correcting factor of the occupations on a Slater determinant (arXiv:2008.00118), modulus–phase networks (Szabó & Castelnovo), RBM times pair product (PRB 96, 205152) and determinant-free signs (arXiv:2108.08631). It found no phase carrier fed by Green's-function ratios. The adoption wall of phase networks is poor generalization. The mechanism offered against it is that the inputs already separate the wrong signs (held-out AUC 0.93). The route is not Route 4: its inputs are non-local, so it is not a correlator product state, and it is trained variationally, not on oracle labels.
    * Gate 1: PASS. The carrier is diagonal in $\mathbf n$.
    * Gate 2: PASS as a design. The foil is t1e's matched `wid`, which has 145 added parameters against 57, so the comparison leans toward KILL. The attribution control is `rpcO`, and the premise ceiling is `free`, an unrestricted phase on each configuration.
    * Gate 3: PASS, sign half only.
    * Gate 4: the parameter count does not depend on $V$, and $\varphi$ is a sum of per-site terms, so it is extensive. The cost is the foil plus the DRF feature gather (a constant factor) plus $\mathcal O(VK)$.
    * *T0 lemma.* At $\varphi=0$ the gradient vanishes identically, because $\mathrm{Re}\,\langle ig\psi\rvert H-E\rvert\psi\rangle=0$ for real $\psi$. The second-order change is $\varepsilon^2q(g)$, with $q(g)=-\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{nm}\psi_n\psi_mH_{nm}(g_n-g_m)^2$, which is negative only along phase patterns whose differences sit on frustrated bonds. So the real foil is a saddle or a minimum in phase space, and that decides the family.
    * *Verdict*: SURVIVED to T1.
* **Route 9.2, LRK-PN: the diagnostic's carrier in the projected, capacity-limited regime (Operation A from U1).**
    * *Ansatz.* LRK unchanged, on a **narrow** projected foil (NNBF width 2, 530 parameters). The narrow foil restores a target on ED-size toys at a capacity-to-difficulty ratio closer to the $4\times4$ instance, where projected width 6 leaves 0.012.
    * Gate 0: as for LRK, the diagnostic's own carrier. Only the regime is new.
    * Gate 1: PASS.
    * Gate 2: PASS. The foil `widN` is the narrow foil widened to 675 parameters, against 650 for LRK. $P/d_{S\chi}\approx0.16$, which is outside the sector-fitting regime.
    * Gate 3: PASS, sign half only ($t'=-0.25$, doped, 6×2 and 4×3).
    * Gate 4: the carrier has 120 parameters at every $V$, and its cost is a constant factor.
    * *Verdict*: SURVIVED to T1.
* **Route 9.3, node optimization on a fixed-node objective (Operation B.3, self-healing DMC transferred to the lattice; built from I3).** KILLED at Gate 0. It is self-healing DMC (Reboredo, Hood & Kent, arXiv:0808.3417), and a 2026 assessment shows that it converges to a wrong node in any finite basis (arXiv:2609.01301). Its lattice form needs FN-GFMC with the network as guide, which is the P1.1 Gate 0 kill (Loehr & Clark, arXiv:2510.26906).
* **Route 9.4, converged-horizon or equal-wall-time LRK (Operation A from U2).** KILLED at T0 under the value-of-information rule.
    * Going past 300 steps at ED size moves the width arm toward sector fitting.
    * The equal-CPU arm `widNL` below already shows that extra steps give width most of LRK's gain.
    * A tie is predicted with probability at least 0.8, and a tie leaves the decision unchanged.
    * The evidence that would overturn this prediction is a compiled re-timing that puts the LRK step cost below twice the width step cost.
* **Not reopened.** U4 (exact ratios, Route 8.2) stays killed by the inheritance clause, because the frozen features it would improve on already carry AUC 0.93 and did not convert. U5 is T2 scope, so it comes only after a T1 PASS-W.
#### 3. T1 results
* **Validity** (`t1f_checks_output.txt`). The copied pass 1 reproduces the foil exactly (relative difference 0). The kernel gradient matches finite differences to $1\times10^{-10}$. $E[\varphi=0]$ equals the foil energy. The gradient at $\varphi=0$ is exactly 0, which confirms the T0 lemma. Every LRK-PN warm start reproduces the projected foil to below $10^{-10}$, and the t1c gap asserts pass ($\ge0.02t$).
* **RPC** (`t1f_verdict_output.txt`; medians of foil seeds 11 and 22; $r=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\mathrm{wid}]$):

| size | foil | `wid` | `free` (ceiling) | $r_{\mathrm{free}}$ | `rpc` | $r$ | counted |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 0 (4×2) | 0.00401 | 0.00168 | 0.00189 | 1.12 | 0.00401 | 2.38 | no |
| 1 (6×2) | 0.01107 | 0.00850 | 0.00999 | 1.17 | 0.01107 | 1.30 | yes |
| 2 (4×3) | 0.04962 | 0.04267 | 0.03272 | 0.77 | 0.04962 | 1.16 | yes |

* **RPC verdict: KILL (PREMISE-OK).** In every run the kernel returned to a phase with $\lvert\cos\varphi\rvert\ge0.996$ and **zero** flipped weight, so RPC equals the foil. The unrestricted phase does descend, flipping 1.7% of the weight at 4×3, and beats width there ($r_{\mathrm{free}}=0.77$). It does not beat width at 6×2.
* **Phase-family premise** (`t1f_curv.py`, written after the RPC kill and before its own run). The quantity is $\lambda(S)$, the minimum over the span $S$ of $q(g)/\mathrm{var}_{\psi^2}(g)$, with medians over seeds:

```
median size 0: ratio +0.1777 ratio2 +0.1251 occ +0.1966 rand +2.5428 free_dir -1.7873
median size 1: ratio +0.3522 ratio2 +0.2632 occ +0.3730 rand +4.4720 free_dir -1.7642
median size 2: ratio +0.3051 ratio2 +0.2173 occ +0.3341 rand +4.4861 free_dir -1.6969
VERDICT phase-family second-order premise: FAMILY-KILL; CPU 11 s
```

* **Reading.**
    * The trained foil is a saddle in phase space. Descending directions exist: the unrestricted direction gives about $-1.7$.
    * But every phase linear or quadratic in the per-site ratio features, without weight sharing ($15V$ columns), has $\lambda>0$ at every size.
    * The ratio features lower $\lambda$ only a little relative to occupations (0.22 against 0.33 at 4×3).
    * So the descent directions are configuration-specific and are not available to any extensive phase carrier built on these features. That is why RPC returned to $\varphi\approx0$.
    * **The phase family is KILLED at T1.**
* **LRK-PN** (`t1g_foilN_output.txt`, `t1g_verdict_output.txt`). The narrow projected foil has a target at both sizes ($\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ 0.0095 and 0.0159), so U1 is testable for the first time.

| size | foilN | `widN` (300 steps) | `lrkN` (300 steps) | $r$ | `widNL` (1150 steps, equal CPU) | $q=\mathrm{lrkN}/\mathrm{widNL}$ |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| 1 (6×2) | 0.00948 | 0.00503 | 0.00281 | 0.56 | 0.00313 | 0.90 |
| 2 (4×3) | 0.01591 | 0.00862 | 0.00882 | 1.02 | — | — |

* **LRK-PN verdict: UNDECIDED** under RULE N, and PASS-W is out of reach.
    * At 6×2, LRK wins clearly at equal steps, and both seeds are separated (0.0027 and 0.0029 against 0.0045 and 0.0056). Its $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ is lower too (0.028 to 0.030 against 0.042 to 0.044), so the gain is general, not specific to the sign.
    * At 4×3 it ties ($r=1.02$, with both seeds within the foil's spread).
    * The equal-CPU arm (amendment written before it ran; LRK costs 3.8 times as much per numpy step) closes most of the 6×2 gap. $q=0.90$ sits on the COST-ARTEFACT edge, which is MIXED by the rule.
    * Since 4×3 already ties at equal steps, no seed extension or compiled re-timing can produce $r\le0.7$ at every counted size. The route stops at T1.
* **Budget.** The ledger total is 1488 CPU-s (24.8 CPU-minutes) of the 30-minute cap, all on one thread. The largest invocation used 416 CPU-s, which is under the 10-minute cap, and every heavy part was gated by a timed projection first.
#### 4. T2
No route passed T1, so there is no value-of-information note and no T2 design (Stage 3.5.4 needs a T1 pass first).
#### 5. Recommendation: STAY-CLOSED
The new evidence adds four things to the diagnostic:
* **Barrier (b) is not what binds.** A carrier that changes the sign without any zero crossing (a phase) fails for a different reason. At the trained foil, the descending phase directions exist but lie outside every extensive span of the ratio features ($\lambda$ from $+0.13$ to $+0.35$). The obstruction is the **non-locality of the needed sign correction in feature space**, not the zero-crossing barrier. The diagnostic's "minimal extra structure" (I1) should now read: *a carrier whose function class contains the configuration-specific descent directions*. Weight-shared per-site kernels on occupations and ratios do not.
* **Unrestricted phases are a finite-size device.** They beat width at 4×3 ($r_{\mathrm{free}}=0.77$) but use $D$ parameters, and they lose at 6×2. So that gain fails Gate 4.
* **The diagnostic now holds in the projected regime too (U1), up to a learning-speed effect on one geometry.** On narrow projected foils, LRK ties width at 4×3. At 6×2 it beats width per step ($r=0.56$), but only by 10% at equal CPU time ($q=0.90$).
* **Scope is unchanged.** All the evidence is at ED size ($V\le12$, $t'=-0.25$, $J=0.4$). The $4\times4$ target is untested by any carrier in this family.
* **What would reopen the leaf.** A carrier whose function class provably contains the free-phase descent direction while keeping a parameter count that does not depend on $V$. A cheap test is $\lambda(S)<0$ in `t1f_curv.py` for a candidate feature span $S$, at both counted sizes, before any training.

## Route Register
Written 2026-11-17 from the rounds above; one card per route ever generated in this leaf, in the order generated. Ids follow the leaf's own labels (Route 1 is R1, Route 2.2 is R2.2, Route 6.7F is R6.7F); routes the leaf left unnumbered get the next free number in their round and say so. Re-audits of an existing route (Routes 3.1, 3.2, 6.1) are folded into the card of the route they re-audit.

Definitions used by every card, so that each card can be read alone:
* **Instances.** The $t$–$t'$–$J$ model (electron hopping $t=1$ between nearest neighbours and $t'$ between next-nearest neighbours, spin exchange $J$, no double occupancy) or the Hubbard model (on-site repulsion $U$) on $V$ sites with $N_\uparrow+N_\downarrow$ electrons. "ED" is exact diagonalization. The **4×4 instance** is the open $4\times4$ $t$–$t'$–$J$ cluster with $6+6$ electrons, $t'=-0.25$, $J=0.4$ (1,681,680 configurations). The **toys** are 2-leg or 3-leg $t$–$t'$–$J$ ladders with $V=8$ to $12$ sites and two holes; a toy size is "counted" only if the foil leaves sign error at least $0.005t$, and from Round 8 on only if its gap $E_1-E_0\ge0.02t$.
* **Ansätze.** NNBF (neural-network backflow) is a Slater determinant whose orbital matrix is the output of a one-hidden-layer network fed the occupations $\mathbf n$; its hidden-layer size is its "width". UHF is the unrestricted Hartree–Fock start. SR (stochastic reconfiguration) is the natural-gradient optimiser; here it uses exact sums over all configurations unless a card says "sampled".
* **Metrics.** $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ is an exact upper bound on the energy error of the best non-negative amplitudes placed under the ansatz's sign (P1.1's `budget()`); $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ is the ansatz's total energy error; $W$ is the ground-state weight on configurations whose sign is wrong. The **foil** is an NNBF made wider until it has the route's parameter count and trained with the same recipe (`nnbf6`, `nnbfW`, `wide`, `wid` in the code). $r=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\text{route}]/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\text{foil}]$ (written $\rho_{\mathrm{sign}}$ in Rounds 2 to 5), a median over seeds; $r<1$ means the route has less sign error.
* **Ratios and votes.** For an edge $e$ (a single hop or a spin exchange) taking $\mathbf n$ to $\mathbf m_e$ with signed Hamiltonian element $c_e$, the ratio is $R_e=c_e\Phi(\mathbf m_e)/\Phi(\mathbf n)$ for a reference $\Phi$. Because $\psi_0(\mathbf n)(E_0-H_{\mathbf n\mathbf n})=\sum_{\mathbf m}H_{\mathbf n\mathbf m}\psi_0(\mathbf m)$, the sum of ratios is a "vote" on the sign at $\mathbf n$. "Exact" ratios use the neighbour's own amplitude; "frozen" ratios keep the backflowed orbitals of $\mathbf n$ and change only the occupied rows (one Green's-function entry each). $E_{\mathrm{act}}\propto V$ is the number of active edges at a configuration. $P_\chi$ projects onto a point-group irrep and $P_S$ onto total spin $S$.
* **Codes.** Gates G0 (novelty and adoption), G1 (conservation and asymptotics), G2 (a cheap baseline matches it), G3 (works only without the Hard Core), G4 (gain or cost does not survive growing $V$); tiers T0 (argument), T1 (toy computation under 10 CPU-minutes per invocation), T2 (one-seed pilot), T3 (multi-seed decisive test); CRITIC (a critic objection not tied to one gate).

### R1 — Jordan–Wigner sign times a learned sign network (Route 1) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Drop the determinant and write the sign as the fixed Jordan–Wigner ordering sign of the site-ordered basis times a learned sign or phase network over occupations (optionally times a fixed determinant), hoping that a network learns the residual nodes directly, without any orbital anchor.
* **Lineage**: initial batch (Round 1, 2026-11-10); not mutated.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-10).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is it new, and if known, does it fix the reason it is not standard practice?
* **Evidence**: The construction is published as a determinant-free exchange sign plus a feed-forward network (Inui, Kato & Motome, arXiv:2108.08631), as a complex restricted Boltzmann machine times a pair product (Nomura et al., PRB 96, 205152 (2017)), and in second-quantized chemistry (Choo et al., Nat. Commun. 11, 2368 (2020)).
* **Why this kills it**: Known methods are admitted only with a mechanism against their adoption barrier. Here the barrier is that the 2D Jordan–Wigner string is non-local and generic sign networks generalise poorly from samples (Szabó & Castelnovo, PRR 2, 033075); the route proposes nothing against either.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a sign network with an input or inductive bias that does generalise, such as the ratio-fed carriers of R9.0 and R9.1.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: newer sign networks may generalise better than the cited ones, so "generalises poorly" may be dated. Not raised by a critic; unresolved, but every later sign carrier in this leaf that did have a structured input still tied width.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed (the round-1 critic's items 5 and 6 concern R4 only).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a relabelling kill under the master's global "re-labelled sign networks" anti-pattern.

### R2 — Pairwise antisymmetric product (Route 2) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Replace the determinant by a product of pair functions $\prod_{i<j}f(x_i,x_j)$ times a symmetric factor, a generalized Vandermonde form costing $\mathcal O(N_e^2)$ instead of $\mathcal O(N_e^3)$, hoping the pair function can place nodes without a mean-field orbital anchor.
* **Lineage**: initial batch (Round 1, 2026-11-10); a configuration-dependent $f$ would reduce it to R1.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G1 (2026-11-10).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 1: does it respect the symmetries and the complexity bounds, here, can its sign class even contain mean-field nodes?
* **Evidence**: With a configuration-independent $f$, the sign in the site-ordered basis is $(-1)^{\sum_{i<j}b_{ij}n_in_j}$ with $b_{ij}\in\{0,1\}$, a quadratic polynomial over GF(2). On a $3\times4$ open lattice with 4 free spinless fermions at $t'=-0.3$, the exact sign on the 396 nonzero configurations is not any such polynomial (`pairwise_check` prints `False`).
* **Why this kills it**: a family that cannot reproduce free-fermion nodes cannot strictly contain the foil's signs (solution condition (i) of section 1); making $f$ configuration-dependent restores universality but turns the route into R1.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: pair products with configuration-dependent pair functions on top of a determinant (that is backflow-like and covered by other routes).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: one 12-site free-fermion check, not a proof for all lattices. Not raised by a critic; the algebraic argument (GF(2) quadratic signs) covers every size, so the check only illustrates it.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a fixed-pair-function sign class is too small; a property of this construction only.

### R3 — Slave-fermion holon determinant times the Marshall sign (Route 3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Use the slave-fermion sign $s=\kappa(\mathbf n)M(\mathbf n)\,\mathrm{sgn}\det X_{[\text{holes}]}$, with $\kappa$ a hole-position gauge, $M=(-1)^{N_\downarrow(A)}$ the Marshall sign ($N_\downarrow(A)$ down spins on sublattice $A$) and $X$ a small holon orbital matrix. It costs $\mathcal O(N_h^3)$ for $N_h$ holes and is not an electron mean field, so it might avoid the electron anchor.
* **Lineage**: initial batch (Round 1, 2026-11-10); revived with holon backflow as R6.5.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G2 (2026-11-10).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 2: does the foil (optimized Slater or BCS sign) already match or beat it?
* **Evidence**: On a $3\times4$ open lattice with 2 holes and $t'=0$, the minimum of $W[Mf]$ over **all** hole-only functions $f$ is 0.0001 at $J/t=100$, 0.0100 at $J/t=10$, 0.195 at $J/t=2$ and 0.342 at $J/t=0.4$; the foil's optimized Slater sign has $W=0.073$ at $J/t=0.4$ (`holon_check`, `kill_evidence_output.txt`).
* **Why this kills it**: the bound covers every holon determinant with any hole-only backflow, so at the physical $J/t=0.4$ the whole family is wrong on about 4.7 times more weight than the foil. The cause is the phase string, which ties the sign to joint spin and hole positions. It is good only as $J/t\to\infty$, where the Marshall sign already cures the sign problem (Gate 3 also fails).
* **What the kill does not rule out**: anchors that use spin-dependent hole functions (R6.5) or a slave-particle sign as a feature rather than as the sign.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: one 12-site cluster at $t'=0$; at $t'\ne0$ the comparison could change. Not raised by a critic; unresolved, but $t'\ne0$ adds frustration that the Marshall sign does not capture, so it should only worsen the anchor.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — the anchor quality of one slave-particle construction.

### R4 — Residual Walsh sign layer on the foil determinant (Route 4) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Keep the foil's determinant sign and multiply it by a learned sign $c(\mathbf n)=\mathrm{sgn}(b+\sum_Sw_S\chi_S(\mathbf n))$ built from Walsh characters $\chi_S$ of degree at most 3 inside $2\times2$ plaquettes, smoothed to a phase in training. The hypothesis was that the foil's residual sign error is local and low-degree, so a cheap local layer fixes it without moving orbitals.
* **Lineage**: initial batch (Round 1, 2026-11-10), the only Round-1 survivor of the gates; after its kill it mutated into R2.1, then R2.3 and R2.2.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-11, by the round-1 critic).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0 after the critic: a local sign-capable diagonal correlator times a Slater or BCS reference is the published correlator-product-state (CPS) times Slater ansatz, so it must fix CPS's adoption barrier; and the pre-registered test must not depend on oracle labels.
* **Evidence**: The smoke preview ($3\times4$ open, $t'=-0.3$, layer fitted by logistic regression to the exact residual sign on 1500 samples) lowered $W$ from 0.073 to 0.072 and raised the fixed-node energy: $M_{\mathrm{local}}=1.03$ against $M_{\mathrm{null}}=1.15$ for a non-local control (both above the FAIL threshold 0.9). The critic added two structural objections: one threshold over an extensive sum cannot express a product of independent local corrections, and the layer is CPS times Slater (Changlani et al., PRB 80, 245116 (2009)).
* **Why this kills it**: it is a known ansatz with no fix for its known barrier (local occupation factors cannot see where the reference's nodes are), its single threshold cannot represent independent local repairs, and its only evidence used labels from $\psi_0$, which VMC does not have.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a product of local factors conditioned on the reference's own amplitudes (R2.2), and residual layers trained by the energy (R8.3).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the decisive $4\times4$ run was never made; the preview was a reduced 12-site run. Not raised by a critic; the structural objections hold at any size, and the preview pointed the same way.
* **Checked by**: round-1 per-leaf critic (2026-11-10, items 5 and 6).
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: A sign layer that sees only local occupations cannot locate a reference's nodes, because the same local pattern is right-signed in one region and wrong-signed in another; sign corrections must be conditioned on the reference's own amplitudes.

### R5 — Neural Pfaffian of a learned pairing matrix (Route 5) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Use the Pfaffian of a configuration-dependent pairing matrix produced by a network, so the antisymmetric object is a pairing state with learned, non-mean-field structure rather than a backflow Slater determinant, hoping pairing nodes are less anchored.
* **Lineage**: initial batch (Round 1, 2026-11-10); not mutated.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-10).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is it new, and does it fix the reason it is not standard?
* **Evidence**: Neural-network-augmented Pfaffians (Chen et al., arXiv:2507.10705) and Neural Pfaffians (Gao & Günnemann, arXiv:2405.14762) are this construction.
* **Why this kills it**: a relabelling; its nodes still move only by deforming a pairing matrix through zero, the same orbital-anchored barrier as backflow.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: Pfaffians as the base of a sign layer that adds a new mechanism.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the lattice $t$–$J$ use may differ from the continuum work. Not raised by a critic; unresolved, but no mechanism was offered.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a relabelling kill.

### R2.1 — Product of plaquette sign factors times the foil (Route 2.1) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Answer the round-1 critic's first objection by replacing one threshold with a product of independent plaquette-local sign or phase factors $\prod_pc_p(\mathbf n_p)$ times the Slater–Jastrow reference, trained by SR from the energy, so independent local sign repairs can multiply.
* **Lineage**: Operation A.1 from R4 after its G0 kill (Round 2, 2026-11-11); its failure fed R2.3 and R2.2.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-11).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is it new, and does it fix the reason it is not standard?
* **Evidence**: it is CPS times Slater (Changlani et al., PRB 80, 245116; Neuscamman et al., PRB 84, 205132 (2011)) and, with complex factors, a complex restricted Boltzmann machine times a pair product (Nomura et al., PRB 96, 205152).
* **Why this kills it**: local occupation factors cannot know where the reference's nodes are, which is why CPS signs have not replaced backflow; this route adds nothing against that.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: local factors whose inputs include the reference's amplitude ratios (R2.2).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: CPS was never tested as a sign fixer on doped $t'\ne0$ clusters. Not raised by a critic; unresolved.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: A sign layer that sees only local occupations cannot locate a reference's nodes, because the same local pattern is right-signed in one region and wrong-signed in another; sign corrections must be conditioned on the reference's own amplitudes.

### R2.2 — FRD-K, factorized-ratio dressing on hop edges of a Slater–Jastrow reference (Routes 2.2 and 3.1) (MERGED)
* **The idea**: Multiply a Slater–Jastrow reference $\Phi$ by $\prod_e[1+\alpha_e(\mathbf n)g(R_e)]$ over all hop edges, with $g(R)=R/\sqrt{1+R^2}$ and $\alpha_e$ from a network. A factor turns negative, flipping $\Phi$'s sign at $\mathbf n$, only when the Hamiltonian neighbour across $\Phi$'s node outvotes $\Phi$, so the eigen-equation vote is built into the ansatz at roughly determinant cost.
* **Lineage**: Operation A.1 from R2.1, R2.3 and R2.4 (Round 2, 2026-11-11); re-audited in Round 3 as Route 3.1 (size-consistency claim withdrawn; sign capability only in the nonlinear regime $\alpha_eg<-1$); its 4×3-torus test was cancelled after pilot 410216 returned NO-TARGET (the projected control left $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}\le10^{-5}$); ported onto an NNBF base as R5.1, and its old design was killed as R5.3.
* **Outcome**: MERGED into R5.1.

### R2.3 — Linear first-order bond dressing (Route 2.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Condition the correction on the reference by applying a linear dressing $\langle\mathbf n\rvert(1+\sum_b\alpha_b(\mathbf n)\hat K_b)\lvert\Phi\rangle$, where $\hat K_b$ hops across bond $b$ and $\alpha_b$ comes from a network, so each configuration's amplitude mixes in its neighbours' reference amplitudes and can change sign.
* **Lineage**: Operation A.1 from R2.1 (Round 2, 2026-11-11); its kill fed R2.2 ("make it a product").
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-11).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0 (known; adoption barrier fixed?) and Gate 1 (asymptotics).
* **Evidence**: with uniform $\alpha$ it is the one-step Lanczos or power step (Sorella, PRB 64, 024512 (2001); Becca et al., arXiv:1412.2656; used on NNBF by Loehr & Clark, arXiv:2510.26906). arXiv:1412.2656 states that a fixed number of Lanczos steps is not size-consistent.
* **Why this kills it**: the correction is a sum of $\mathcal O(V)$ terms against a fixed 1, so its relative effect on an intensive quantity vanishes as $V\to\infty$; a configuration-dependent $\alpha$ does not change that.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: multiplicative (product) versions of the same neighbour information (R2.2).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: at ED sizes a linear step can still help. Not raised by a critic; true but irrelevant, since the Hard Core concerns growing $V$.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: A linear dressing $(1+\sum_b\alpha_b\hat K_b)\Phi$ is not size-consistent at a fixed number of steps, whatever its coefficients, so any neighbour-amplitude correction must enter multiplicatively.

### R2.4 — Brick layer of two-site fermionic gates, the lattice Baeriswyl projector (Route 2.4) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Apply a brick layer of configuration-dependent two-site gates $\prod_be^{\theta_b(\mathbf n)\hat K_b}$ to the reference, the lattice analogue of the Baeriswyl kinetic projector $e^{-\lambda\hat T}$, so kinetic correlations move the nodes the way a short imaginary-time evolution would.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.3, cross-field translation from fermionic tensor-network gate layers and the Baeriswyl projector (Round 2, 2026-11-11); its kill fed R2.2 ("keep only the first-order, single-bond content and factorize it").
* **Source analogy**: In tensor networks and in Baeriswyl or Gutzwiller–Baeriswyl variational states (Hetényi, Tanatar & Martelo, PRB 93, 174518 (2016), arXiv:1605.02698), a layer of local gates or a kinetic projector $e^{-\lambda\hat T}$ acts on a reference. Correspondence: gate layer ↔ sign-changing dressing; auxiliary branch configurations ↔ sampled intermediate states. It works for bosons because the branch sum is positive; the gap is that for interacting fermionic gates the branch sum carries signs.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G1 (2026-11-11).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 1: is the amplitude computable in polynomial time without a new sign problem?
* **Evidence**: with interacting gates the exact amplitude is a signed sum over $\mathcal O(2^{N_b})$ branches ($N_b$ bonds); sampling the branches (the Hetényi or off-diagonal-wave-function route) reintroduces a sign problem; with quadratic gates the layer is an orbital rotation and adds nothing. The uniform-$\lambda$ form is also published (Gate 0).
* **Why this kills it**: either exponential cost, a new sign problem, or no new expressivity.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a factorized, first-order version (R2.2), and gate layers on bosonic or stoquastic problems.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: a shallow layer with few non-commuting gates could be summed exactly for small depth. Not raised by a critic; unresolved, but one layer already has $2^{N_b}$ branches.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — the branch-sum sign problem of fermionic gate layers is textbook, not a finding of this run.

### R2.5 — FRD-X, factorized-ratio dressing on spin-exchange edges (Routes 2.5 and 3.2) (MERGED)
* **The idea**: The same factorized-ratio dressing as R2.2, but with edges that are the spin exchanges $\hat S^+_i\hat S^-_j$ of the large-$U$ (Schrieffer–Wolff) effective Hamiltonian, so the votes come from configurations in the same doublon sector, where the low-energy spin sign structure is decided, instead of from poorly converged doublon amplitudes.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.1, frame change (Round 2, 2026-11-11); re-audited as Route 3.2 in Round 3; ported onto an NNBF base as R5.2.
* **Outcome**: MERGED into R5.2.

### R2.6 — Backflow in a learned rotated orbital frame (Route 2.6) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Sample and run backflow in a learned single-particle basis $\hat c^\dagger_k=\sum_iU_{ik}\hat c^\dagger_i$ (for example momentum or natural orbitals), so the nodes are defined in a basis where the sign structure may be simpler.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.1, frame change (Round 2, 2026-11-11); not mutated.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-11).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is it new, and does it fix the reason it is not standard?
* **Evidence**: orbital rotations co-optimized with NQS (Robledo Moreno et al., arXiv:2302.11588) and basis optimization for hidden-fermion states (Gauvin-Ndiaye et al., PRL 134, 076502 (2025)) are published; a rotated Hubbard interaction has $\mathcal O(N_\uparrow N_\downarrow V^2)$ connected elements per sample.
* **Why this kills it**: known, and its adoption wall (the dense local energy of a rotated two-body term, leaf P2.2) is not addressed.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: rotations restricted to keep the interaction sparse.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: a partial rotation might keep most sparsity. Not raised by a critic; unresolved.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — inherits P2.2's cost wall.

### R3.0 — Local-energy Jastrow, the first-order limit of FRD (Route 3.0) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Re-gate FRD against its first-order expansion: for small ratios $\prod_e[1+\alpha_eg(R_e)]\approx\exp(\sum_e\alpha_eR_e)$, which with uniform $\alpha_e=-\lambda$ is $\Phi e^{-\lambda E^{\mathrm{kin}}_{\mathrm{loc}}}$, a Jastrow factor of the reference's own kinetic local energy, checking whether this regime alone could carry the sign gain.
* **Lineage**: Round-3 re-gating of R2.2 after the round-2 critic's size-consistency objection (2026-11-11); its kill moved FRD's claim to the nonlinear regime and later seeded AO-F (R6.3).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G2 (2026-11-11).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 2: can it change the sign, or is it in the foil's sign class?
* **Evidence**: $\exp(\cdot)>0$ at every $\mathbf n$, so $\mathrm{sgn}\,\psi=\mathrm{sgn}\,\Phi$; it also differs from the Baeriswyl state at order $\lambda^2$ by the local variance of the hopping.
* **Why this kills it**: P1.1 found the sign to be the binding error, and a positive factor cannot change any sign.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: positive factors that act through a symmetry projection or by freeing backflow capacity (tested and killed as R6.3 and R7.1).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: under $P_\chi$ a positive factor can move projected nodes. Raised by the worker in Round 6 (R6.3) and tested there; it lost to width.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a positive factor has the reference's sign by construction.

### R3.3 — Projected-reference FRD (Route 3.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Answer the round-2 critic's symmetry objection by building every FRD ratio from the symmetry-projected reference, $R_e=c_e(P_\chi\Phi)(\mathbf m_e)/(P_\chi\Phi)(\mathbf n)$, so the votes are cast by a state that already has the right point-group symmetry.
* **Lineage**: Operation A.2 on the round-2 critic's symmetry objection to R2.2 (Round 3, 2026-11-11).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G1 (2026-11-11).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 1: does it keep the locality and asymptotics the route needs?
* **Evidence**: each ratio needs $\lvert G\rvert$ determinants ($\lvert G\rvert$ the group order), and every factor then depends on the whole group orbit of $\mathbf n$, so a translated image of a distant region enters every factor.
* **Why this kills it**: it loses even the approximate multiplicative separability that made FRD's correction local, while projecting the whole FRD state, $P_SP_\chi\psi$, reaches the symmetry goal at the same cost.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: projecting the full state (what the round-3 and round-5 tests did).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: on small clusters locality matters little. Not raised by a critic; true, but the route then has nothing over full projection.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — dominated by projecting the whole state.

### R5.1 — NNBF×FRD-K, hop-edge vote factors on a neural backflow base (Routes 5.1, ported from R2.2) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Put FRD-K's factors $\prod_e[1+\alpha_e(\mathbf n)g(R_e)]$ on a width-4 NNBF base $\Phi$, with exact neighbour ratios from the base's own backflow, so that the base moves nodes by deforming orbitals and the factors add per-configuration sign flips wherever a hole-hop neighbour across the base's node outvotes it.
* **Lineage**: ported from R2.2 by Operation A.2 on round-3 critic item (a) (Round 5, 2026-11-12); its cost wall seeded R5.4 (FRD-F); its VOTE-INERT read-out seeded R6.3 (AO-F) and R6.7.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T2 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: pre-registered T2-a rule (`t2_readout.py`): with $v=(\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\lvert F\rvert]-\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}^{K})/\mathrm{gap}$, where $\lvert F\rvert$ removes every flip, "VOTE-INERT if $v<0.25$, VOTE-CARRIES if $v\ge0.5$"; and the T3 final rule "PASS iff 5-seed $\rho_{\mathrm{sign}}\le0.7$, at least 4 of 5 seeds below the control median, $\rho_{\mathrm{tot}}\le1.0$, $\kappa\le10$, MC_OK; else FAIL".
* **Evidence**: on the projected 4×4 instance, 3 seeds (job 451916), FRD-K (1719 parameters) against NNBF width 6 (1734): $\rho_{\mathrm{sign}}=0.84$ ($\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ median 0.0101 against 0.0120), $\rho_{\mathrm{tot}}=0.92$, 3 of 3 paired wins, AMBIGUOUS. Removing every flip from the trained state moved $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ from 0.009751 to 0.009757 ($v=0.003$, job 697584); flip weight at most $3.7\times10^{-3}$ in all six runs. With all ten control runs stored, the 5-seed $\rho_{\mathrm{sign}}\ge0.782>0.7$ whatever seeds 44 and 55 gave. Cost: $(1+E_{\mathrm{act}})$ times NNBF's passes per local energy (522 against 32 at $L=4$, 1867 against 62 at $L=6$).
* **Why this kills it**: the route's novelty is a sign-capable vote, and the trained state does not use it ($v=0.003$), so its 16% gain is an amplitude effect; the decisive rule could no longer pass; and its cost overhead grows as $\mathcal O(V)$, so a matched-parameter win cannot become a matched-compute win (G4 cost fails too).
* **What the kill does not rule out**: votes that are used (tested as R6.7 and R8.1), and the amplitude effect itself (tested as R6.3).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: VOTE-INERT reads one SR-trained state, not the architecture; a different optimiser might use the flips (round-6 critic objection 2). Answered only partly: the projected vote applied without training was usable on that state ($z=-1.33$, job 698178), but every route that tried to train or cheapen it died (R6.7, R6.7F, R8.1).
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, objections 1, 2 and 8).
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: A sign layer can win a matched-parameter test without using its sign capacity, so every sign-layer gain needs a flips-off read-out before it is credited to the sign mechanism; LESSON?: Before planning branches on a pending job, check from stored data whether its pre-registered rule can still pass.

### R5.2 — NNBF×FRD-X, exchange-edge vote factors on a neural backflow base (Routes 5.2, ported from R2.5) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: The same vote factors as R5.1, but on spin-exchange edges (48 on the 4×4 instance), where in $t$–$J$ the exchange is part of $H$ itself; the hope was that exchange votes, which stay within one hole configuration, carry the spin sign structure more reliably than hole hops.
* **Lineage**: ported from R2.5 (Round 5, 2026-11-12); its failure seeded R6.2 (FRD-H, all edges).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T3 (2026-11-12, job 451916).
* **Kill rule**: pre-registered: "FAIL if $\rho_{\mathrm{sign}}\ge0.9$, or $\rho_{\mathrm{tot}}>1.1$, or $\kappa>10$, or MC_FAIL", with $\kappa$ the ratio of local-energy kurtosis to the control's.
* **Evidence**: 3 seeds on the projected 4×4 instance against NNBF width 6 (1656 against 1734 parameters): $\rho_{\mathrm{sign}}=1.29$, $\rho_{\mathrm{tot}}=1.24$, $\kappa=11.9$.
* **Why this kills it**: it has more sign error and more total error than the matched foil, and its local-energy tails are 12 times heavier, which would make sampled training noisier still.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: exchange-ratio information used as continuous features (R7.3, later killed for other reasons).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: on the toys exchange votes were *more* informative than hop votes (lift 3.2 against 2.0 at $V=12$), so the failure is trainability, not information. Raised by the Round-6 worker; it confirms rather than overturns the kill.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16), who read the round-5 result files and raised no objection.
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: An informative vote is not a trainable vote: a ratio-based sign factor must be judged by the local-energy tails it adds as well as by the information it carries.

### R5.3 — FRD on a Slater–Jastrow base against an NNBF control (Route 5.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Keep the round-3 design, FRD factors on a Slater–Jastrow reference, and compare it with an NNBF control on the 4×4 instance, testing whether the vote factors alone can do what backflow does by deforming orbitals.
* **Lineage**: the round-3 test design of R2.2, re-examined in Round 5 (2026-11-12); replaced by R5.1.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at CRITIC (2026-11-12).
* **Kill rule**: round-3 critic item (a): the contrast must isolate the sign layer, so FRD must sit on the same base as its control.
* **Evidence**: the design compares "FRD on no backflow" with "backflow", two changes at once.
* **Why this kills it**: a FAIL would say nothing about FRD and a PASS is implausible by construction, so the test cannot change a decision.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: FRD on an NNBF base (R5.1).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: none; the confound is structural.
* **Checked by**: round-3 per-leaf critic (2026-11-12, item a).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a confounded design, already a standard fair-comparison rule.

### R5.4 — FRD-F, frozen-frame ratios to remove FRD's cost wall (Routes 5.4 and 6.1) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Make FRD cost the same as NNBF by taking each ratio from the base's backflowed orbitals frozen at $\mathbf n$, $R^F_e=c_e\det O_{\mathbf n}[\mathrm{rows}(\mathbf m_e)]/\det O_{\mathbf n}[\mathrm{rows}(\mathbf n)]$, one Sherman–Morrison update instead of a full NNBF pass per neighbour.
* **Lineage**: Operation A.1 on R5.1's cost wall (Round 5, held; re-gated as Route 6.1 in Round 6, 2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T2 (2026-11-16, job 697584).
* **Kill rule**: pre-registered T2-b: $u=(\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[F^F]-\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}^{K})/\mathrm{gap}$; "DROP-IN-PASS if $u\le0.25$, KILLED if $u\ge1$".
* **Evidence**: substituting frozen ratios into the trained FRD-K state gave $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}=0.0132$ against 0.00975 for FRD-K and 0.0120 for the control ($u=1.50$), and doubled $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$. Its cost passes Gate 4 (wall-time ratio flat at 2.2, slope $-0.05$). Frozen ratios have median error 23% against exact ones.
* **Why this kills it**: the read-out loses all of FRD-K's gain, and, as the critic re-based it, FRD-F exists only to make the vote cheap, while T2-a showed the vote is unused.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a retrained FRD-F (the drop-in test used weights trained for exact ratios), and frozen ratios as learned features (R7.2).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: a drop-in failure does not show that a retrained FRD-F fails (round-6 critic objection 5). Answered by re-basing the kill on VOTE-INERT.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, objection 5).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — dies with the unused vote it was built to cheapen.

### R6.2 — FRD-H, vote factors on every off-diagonal move (Route 6.2) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Fix FRD-X's narrow vote by putting FRD factors on all off-diagonal moves, hops and exchanges together (132 edges on the 4×4 instance), so each configuration's vote is cast by the full eigen-equation rather than by its minority exchange part.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R5.2's failure (Round 6, 2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: pre-registered R2 in `t1_vote.py`: kill FRD-H if the recall of its summed vote is at most hop-only recall at $V=8$ and $V=10$.
* **Evidence**: on UHF anchors of ladders with $V=8,10,12$, recall was 0.095 against 0.221 and 0.221 against 0.255 at the first two sizes. On the trained 4×4 base, however, recall was 0.380 against 0.347.
* **Why this kills it**: the rule fired as written; the critic kept the kill on two other grounds: it carries 1.6 times R5.1's $\mathcal O(V)$ cost wall, and its vote would be as unused as R5.1's.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: all-edge ratios as continuous features (R7.4).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the recall rule is contradicted at $V=16$ on a trained base (round-6 critic objection 6). Answered by re-basing on Gate 4 cost and VOTE-INERT.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, objection 6).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — inherits R5.1's cost and inert-vote failures.

### R6.3 — AO-F, sign-inert ratio Jastrow on frozen ratios (Route 6.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Since FRD-K's gain was amplitude-borne, keep only that part: $\psi=\Phi_{\mathrm{NNBF}}\exp(\sum_e\alpha_e(\mathbf n)g(R^F_e))$, a positive factor that cannot flip signs itself but may move the projected nodes through image amplitudes in $\sum_g\chi_g\psi(g\mathbf n)$ or free backflow capacity for the nodes.
* **Lineage**: Operation A.3 on R3.0's kill, after R5.1's VOTE-INERT read-out (Round 6, 2026-11-16); sibling AO-L is R7.1.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: pre-registered in `t1_ao.py`: with $r^\ast=\max(r_J,r_W)$, $r_J$ the ratio to a matched NNBF times neural Jastrow and $r_W$ to matched width, "KILL if $r^\ast\ge0.9$ at 2 or more counted sizes, including the largest".
* **Evidence**: unprojected ladders, 300 exact SR steps, seeds 11 and 22; the exact-ratio arm `aoK` (the most informative family member) against width: $r_W=2.04$, 0.95, 1.19 at $V=8,10,12$; against the neural Jastrow it won ($r_J=0.56$, 0.67, 0.48). Any positive factor lost to width ($r_{JW}$ 1.4 to 3.6).
* **Why this kills it**: the ratio features carry real information (they beat a plain Jastrow), but at matched parameters a positive factor loses to backflow width at every size, so the "frees capacity" mechanism is refuted; the projection mechanism is a fixed-$\lvert G\rvert$ finite-size effect (G4).
* **What the kill does not rule out**: the same features entering the orbitals (R7.2), and projected training at larger $V$.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: 2 seeds with large spreads (width's $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ 0.011 and 0.026 at $V=12$), unprojected, 300 steps. Raised by the round-6 critic as caveats; unresolved but the losses are by factors of 1.2 to 2.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, who ran the test; objections 4 and 5).
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: Off-loading amplitude into a positive correlator does not buy sign accuracy at matched parameters; the same parameters spent on backflow width win.

### R6.4 — Complex detour, real part of a complex backflow (Route 6.4) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Train a complex backflow $\psi_c=A+iB$ and read out $\mathrm{Re}\,\psi_c=A$, so a sign can change by rotating the phase instead of driving $\lvert\psi_c\rvert$ through zero, which would remove the zero-crossing barrier.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.1, real-to-complex frame (Round 6, 2026-11-16); repaired as R9.1 (one shared amplitude).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T0 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: T0 lemma at Gate 2: does the objective couple the parts so the readout gains?
* **Evidence**: for real symmetric $H$, $E[\psi_c]=(\langle A\rvert H\lvert A\rangle+\langle B\rvert H\lvert B\rangle)/(\langle A\vert A\rangle+\langle B\vert B\rangle)$, a weighted mean of $E[A]$ and $E[B]$.
* **Why this kills it**: the energy never couples $A$ and $B$ except through shared parameters, so a node crossing in either part still costs that part's own barrier.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a phase on one shared amplitude, $\psi e^{i\varphi}$, where the energy does couple the parts (R9.1).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: shared parameters do couple $A$ and $B$. Not raised by a critic; true but uncontrolled, and the critic sharpened the lemma's wording (objection 9).
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, objection 9).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — superseded by R9.1's broader phase-family result.

### R6.5 — Holon-frame backflow (Route 6.5) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Revive R3 with spin-dependent holon backflow, $\psi=M(\mathbf n)\det X_\theta(\mathbf n)_{[\mathrm{holes}]}A(\mathbf n)$, so the expensive part is a small hole determinant and the anchor is the Marshall sign rather than an electron mean field.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.1, electron-to-holon frame, from R3 (Round 6, 2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G3 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 3: does it work only where the Hard Core is absent?
* **Evidence**: R3's bound: the best Marshall-times-hole-function anchor has $W=0.34$ against 0.073 for the foil Slater at $J/t=0.4$, and is good only for $J/t$ of order 10 or more.
* **Why this kills it**: with full spin-dependent backflow the family is universal, so it reduces to anchor quality, and its anchor is good only in the strong-exchange limit that Anti-Solution Space item (3) excludes.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: holon features as inputs to an electron backflow.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: R3's bound speaks to the start, not to what training reaches (round-6 critic objection 9). Unresolved; the kill stands as a statement about the start.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, objection 9).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — anchor quality of one frame.

### R6.6 — Node-targeted sampling (Route 6.6) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Bias the Monte Carlo sampler toward configurations near the nodes, where the sign gradients live, and reweight, so training concentrates on the configurations whose signs need to change.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.2, active probing (Round 6, 2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T0 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: T0: can a sampler change what SR converges to?
* **Evidence**: every test in this leaf uses exact-sum forces, so no sampler enters; in sampled SR the fixed point does not depend on the sampling distribution, only the variance does (P1.3's sign-flip-aware Metropolis kill).
* **Why this kills it**: it can change noise, not the trained nodes.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: variance reduction near nodes as an efficiency aid.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: with finite samples and a finite step budget, the sampler changes which minimum is reached. Not raised by a critic; unresolved, but P1.3 found no basins to steer between.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, objection 9).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — repeats P1.3's lesson.

### R6.7 — Vote-initialized FRD (Route 6.7) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Start every FRD factor so that it already flips the sign wherever the vote fires (from Round 7 on: the projected exact vote, which on the trained 4×4 state removed 1.33 times FRD-K's sign advantage), then train by SR, so training begins with the vote in use instead of having to discover it.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R5.1's VOTE-INERT result (Round 6, killed at T0 for false alarms); reopened after T2c `gvote` returned VOTE-USABLE ($z=-1.33$, job 698178); killed again in Round 7; mutated into R6.7F and R8.1.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G4 (2026-11-16, Round 7).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 4: does its cost per sample stay a constant multiple of NNBF's as $V$ grows?
* **Evidence**: the exact projected vote at $\mathbf n$ needs $P_\chi\psi(\mathbf m_e)$ on every active edge: $(1+E_{\mathrm{act}})\lvert G\rvert$ base passes per amplitude, R5.1's measured wall (522 against 32 passes at $L=4$) times $\lvert G\rvert=8$. The Round-6 T0 kill: on UHF anchors, applying the raw vote raised $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ at every size (for example 0.108 to 0.166 at $V=12$).
* **Why this kills it**: the cost overhead grows with $V$; the only cheap form is the frozen frame (R6.7F); and the `base` read-out (REFRAME-CARRIES, $b=3.59$) places the 4×4 gain in the point-group projection, a finite-size mechanism.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: an exact vote made cheap by a finite-receptive-field backflow (R8.1).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the projected vote did help once ($z=-1.33$). Unresolved as an accuracy fact; it does not survive cost.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (objection 9) and round-7 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, accepted).
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: Ratio or vote features that read exact neighbour amplitudes cost $(1+E_{\mathrm{act}})$ amplitude evaluations per amplitude, a hidden $\mathcal O(V)$ factor; operation-count the per-sample algorithm at T0 instead of timing on a stored full-basis vector.

### R6.7F — Frozen-frame vote on the trained foil (Route 6.7F) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Make the vote cheap by computing it from frozen ratios, one inverse per amplitude, and flip the trained matched-width foil's sign wherever its own hop vote fires, with no gate and no added parameters, so the comparison with the foil is exact.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R6.7's cost kill (Round 7, 2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: pre-registered in `t1_vote67.py`: with $q$ the median ratio of flipped to unflipped $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$, "KILL iff $q_{\mathrm{frozen}}\ge1.0$ at 2 or more counted sizes including the largest".
* **Evidence**: on unprojected ladders, $q_{\mathrm{frozen}}=5.22$ (uncounted $V=8$), 1.75 ($V=10$), 1.31 ($V=12$); the exact vote, reported only, gave $q_{\mathrm{exact}}=0.81$, 0.90, 0.92.
* **Why this kills it**: the frozen vote raises the foil's sign error by 30% to 75% at the counted sizes.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: the same frozen ratios as learned features (the round-7 critic found them informative, AUC 0.93), and the exact vote (R8.1).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: $V=10$ is near-degenerate (gap $0.0041t$). Raised by the round-7 critic for the DRF kills; here the $V=12$ result alone is above 1.
* **Checked by**: round-7 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, objection 8 and the restated anti-pattern).
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: A fixed thresholded vote on frozen Green's-function ratios of a trained backflow is harmful, while the same ratios used as learned features are informative; do not conclude from a failed hard-coded rule that the information is absent.

### R6.8 — One-shot projected-vote sign read-out (Route 6.8) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Apply the vote once, without training, to the symmetry-projected state, where its lift (precision over base rate) was 129, and read the sign off the result, using the projection to make the vote reliable.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.2 refill after R5.1's VOTE-INERT result (Round 6, 2026-11-16); its cheap local form became R8.1.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is it new, and does it fix the known method's adoption barrier?
* **Evidence**: a sign set by one exact local application of $H$ to a projected state is the sign of a one-step power or Lanczos state (Becca et al., arXiv:1412.2656; Loehr & Clark, arXiv:2510.26906).
* **Why this kills it**: known, and not size-consistent at a fixed number of steps (R2.3's kill).
* **What the kill does not rule out**: votes inside a size-consistent product (R2.2's family).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the selective (thresholded) version is not the plain Lanczos step. Not raised by a critic; the threshold version was later rejected as R8.5 on the same ground.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, objection 9).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a Lanczos-step relabel.

### R7.1 — AO-L, sign-inert ratio Jastrow on exact ratios (Route 7.1) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Drop FRD-K's inert flips and keep $\Phi\exp\sum_e\alpha_eg(R_e)$ on **exact** ratios, made affordable by a finite-receptive-field backflow whose neighbour amplitudes are low-rank updates, so the information that made FRD-K's amplitudes better is kept at constant cost.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R5.1 (round-6 critic refill, 2026-11-16); sibling of R6.3.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: the `t1_ao.py` rule: "KILL if $r^\ast\ge0.9$ at 2 or more counted sizes, including the largest".
* **Evidence**: it is the `aoK` arm of R6.3's test: $r_W=2.04$, 0.95, 1.19 at $V=8,10,12$ against matched width.
* **Why this kills it**: as for R6.3, a positive factor on exact ratios loses to width at every size.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: exact ratios fed into the orbitals (R8.2).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: as for R6.3: 2 seeds, unprojected, 300 steps. Unresolved.
* **Checked by**: round-6 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, who ran it; not reviewed independently).
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: Off-loading amplitude into a positive correlator does not buy sign accuracy at matched parameters; the same parameters spent on backflow width win.

### R7.2 — DRF-K, determinant-ratio-feature backflow on hop edges (Route 7.2) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Move the vote's information into the orbitals: a first NNBF pass gives orbitals whose inverse yields frozen hop ratios; these feed a second backflow pass that outputs the final orbitals. The nodes then move by continuous orbital deformation, avoiding both flips (which SR declined) and positive factors (which lose to width).
* **Lineage**: Operation B.3, cross-field translation from continuum iterative backflow (round-6 critic refill, 2026-11-16); siblings R7.3 and R7.4; warm-start and tied repairs are R8.3 and R8.4; exact-ratio form R8.2.
* **Source analogy**: In continuum quantum Monte Carlo, iterative backflow (Taddei et al., PRB 91, 115106 (2015); network form, Ruggeri et al., arXiv:1711.01993) feeds a first set of backflow coordinates into a second transformation. Correspondence: electron coordinates ↔ occupations; second-iteration input ↔ Green's-function ratios of the first pass. It worked there because the second iteration sees correlated positions the first could not express; the gap is that on a lattice the input is a discrete occupation vector, and generic depth already saturates (Loehr & Clark).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: pre-registered T1b in `t1b_drf.py`: "KILL if $r_W\ge0.9$ at 2 or more counted sizes, including the largest".
* **Evidence**: on unprojected ladders (UHF start, 300 exact SR steps; 3 seeds for this arm), $r_W=1.52$ at $V=10$ and 2.14 at $V=12$ ($\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ 0.0238 against 0.0156, and 0.0200 against 0.0093). Its untrained premise had passed: ratio features added 0.03 to 0.13 of held-out AUC over occupations, rising with $V$.
* **Why this kills it**: the trained second pass has about twice the sign error of the same parameters spent on width at both counted sizes.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a warm-started, wider second pass (R8.3), tied re-entry (R8.4), exact ratios (R8.2). The round-7 critic noted the design gave pass 2 only 2 hidden units and a moving input.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: cold start, narrow pass 2, and a near-degenerate counted size ($V=10$, gap $0.0041t$) where seed 33 alone decided it (round-7 critic Majors 2 and 3). Answered by R8.3, which removed all three confounds and still tied.
* **Checked by**: round-7 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, Majors 2 and 3).
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: An information premise measured on the starting determinant does not predict a trained gain; measure it on the trained foil before building a carrier.

### R7.3 — DRF-X, determinant-ratio-feature backflow on exchange edges (Route 7.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: The same two-pass backflow as R7.2, fed exchange-move ratios (a rank-1 update in each spin determinant), chosen because exchange votes were more informative than hop votes at $V=10$ and 12, and because DRF has no multiplicative factor to create FRD-X's heavy local-energy tails.
* **Lineage**: sibling frame of R7.2 (round-6 critic refill, 2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: the R7.2 T1b rule.
* **Evidence**: 2 seeds: $r_W=2.35$ at $V=10$ and 1.42 at $V=12$; its premise had passed with AUC gains 0.10 to 0.23.
* **Why this kills it**: it loses to width at both counted sizes.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: as for R7.2.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: at $V=12$ the kill is not clean: seed 11 (0.0121) beat foil seed 11 (0.0128); and $V=10$ is near-degenerate (round-7 critic Major 3). Unresolved for this arm alone; R8.3 (all-edge features, warm start) tied.
* **Checked by**: round-7 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, Major 3).
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: A counted toy size must have a spectral gap ($E_1-E_0\ge0.02t$ here); on a near-degenerate toy the wrong-sign labels follow the partner state and one seed decides the verdict.

### R7.4 — DRF-H, determinant-ratio-feature backflow on all edges (Route 7.4) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: The same two-pass backflow as R7.2, fed ratios from every off-diagonal move (hops and exchanges, about 1.6 times R7.2's features at constant cost each), so the second pass sees the whole eigen-equation vote.
* **Lineage**: sibling frame of R7.2 (round-6 critic refill, 2026-11-16); its feature set is the one R8.3, R8.4, R9.0 and R9.1 used.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: the R7.2 T1b rule.
* **Evidence**: 2 seeds: $r_W=1.52$ at $V=10$ and 1.91 at $V=12$; premise AUC gains 0.12 to 0.25.
* **Why this kills it**: it loses to width at both counted sizes.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: as for R7.2.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the same three confounds as R7.2. Answered by R8.3 (same features, warm start, wide pass 2, gapped sizes: tie).
* **Checked by**: round-7 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, Majors 2 and 3).
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: An information premise measured on the starting determinant does not predict a trained gain; measure it on the trained foil before building a carrier.

### R7.5 — Gated mixture of NNBF experts (Route 7.5) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Write $\psi=\sum_k\pi_k(\mathbf n)\Phi_k(\mathbf n)$ with a softmax gate over several NNBF experts, so a sign changes where the gate switches between experts with different nodes, without any single determinant crossing zero.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.3, cross-field translation from machine-learning mixtures of experts (round-6 critic refill, 2026-11-16).
* **Source analogy**: In machine learning, mixtures of experts (Jacobs, Jordan, Nowlan & Hinton, Neural Comput. 3, 79 (1991)) let a gate pick a specialist per input. Correspondence: expert ↔ one NNBF with its own nodes; gate ↔ region of configuration space. It worked there because inputs cluster into a few regimes; the gap is that independent local sign repairs in many regions combine multiplicatively.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G4 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 4: does its cost survive growing $V$?
* **Evidence**: $R$ independent repairs in distant regions need $2^R$ experts, or a gate that factorizes over regions, which is a CPS sign correlator (R2.1's kill). It is also a sum of determinants (Anti-Solution Space item 1).
* **Why this kills it**: the number of experts grows exponentially with the number of independent repairs, which grows with $V$.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a few experts for a few global sectors (for example competing orders), which is not a sign-repair mechanism.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: repairs might not be independent. Not raised by a critic; unresolved.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
