* **Lesson**: route-specific — a multi-determinant sum, already excluded.

### R7.6 — Transcorrelated frame for NNBF (Route 7.6) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Train an NNBF $\Phi$ on the similarity-transformed Hamiltonian $\tilde H=e^{-J}He^{J}$ with a pre-optimised diagonal $J$, so that the backflow only has to describe what the correlator leaves, hoping its nodes then come out better.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.3, cross-field translation from transcorrelated FCIQMC (round-6 critic refill, 2026-11-16).
* **Source analogy**: In quantum chemistry and lattice FCIQMC, the transcorrelated method (Dobrautz, Luo & Alavi, PRB 99, 075119 (2019)) absorbs a Jastrow factor into a non-Hermitian $\tilde H$, which compacts the ground state in the configuration basis. Correspondence: Jastrow ↔ positive correlator; compact CI vector ↔ easier backflow target. It worked because projective methods benefit from compactness; the gap is that a variational ansatz represents $e^J\Phi$ either way.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G2 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 2: does a cheap baseline already reach the same family?
* **Evidence**: on a lattice with diagonal $J$, the represented state $e^J\Phi$ is exactly the NNBF-times-Jastrow family (`nnbfJ`), which lost to width at T1 (R6.3's test, $r_{JW}$ 1.4 to 3.6).
* **Why this kills it**: it is the losing baseline with a non-variational objective; the objective question is P1.3's scope.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: transcorrelation with non-diagonal (off-diagonal) correlators.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: a different objective can reach different minima. Not raised by a critic; unresolved, and P1.3 found no basins.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — reduces to a killed baseline.

### R8.1 — 6.7L, exact vote on a finite-receptive-field backflow (Route 8.1) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Fix R6.7's cost by giving pass 1 a finite receptive field of radius $r$: a hop then changes only nearby orbital rows, so each exact neighbour ratio is a rank-$\mathcal O(r^d)$ determinant-lemma update, and the exact vote costs a constant factor; the factors are initialized to flip where the exact vote fires and trained by SR.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R6.7's G4 kill (Round 7 refill, 2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-16, by the round-7 critic).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0 (relabel) and Gate 4 (finite-size gain): at initialization, is it a known method, and does its gain survive growing $V$?
* **Evidence**: at initialization the ansatz is the trained base times a flip wherever the exact local-energy vote fires, which is R6.8's one-step Lanczos sign; training adds only trainable flips, which SR left unused ($v=0.003$, R5.1); on the trained foil the exact vote's own gain is $q_{\mathrm{exact}}=0.90$ and 0.92 at the counted sizes, at the route's 0.9 kill threshold before training and weakening with size.
* **Why this kills it**: locality fixes R6.7's cost, not R6.8's novelty, and the starting gain is already at the threshold and falling with $V$.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: exact ratios as continuous features (R8.2).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: SR from a vote-initialized start might keep and extend the gain. Not tested; the critic judged it unlikely from R5.1's inert flips.
* **Checked by**: round-7 per-leaf critic (2026-11-16, Major 4).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a cheap form of the R6.8 relabel.

### R8.2 — DRF-E, two-pass backflow fed exact neighbour ratios (Route 8.2) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Keep R7.2's two-pass architecture but swap the frozen ratios for exact ones, made affordable by R8.1's low-rank updates on a local pass 1, on the then-held theory that frozen ratios of a trained backflow were the failing sub-component.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on the R7.2 to R7.4 kills (Round 7 refill, 2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T0 (2026-11-16, Round 8).
* **Kill rule**: the round-7 critic's inheritance clause, written before R8.3 ran: "If 8.3 fails because a second pass cannot convert information that is present, 8.2 inherits that failure."
* **Evidence**: R8.3 failed in exactly that way ($r=0.99$, with features of AUC 0.93); exact features add only 0.03 of AUC over frozen ones at $V=12$ (0.96 against 0.93).
* **Why this kills it**: to reach $r\le0.7$, a small information increment would have to give a 30% sign gain where the larger frozen information gave none.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: exact ratios in a carrier whose function class differs from a second backflow pass.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the kill is by argument, not by a run, and the T1 budget was spent. Not raised by a critic; flagged for the per-leaf critic and not reviewed individually.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed individually; the cross-node critic (2026-11-16, Majors 3 and 4) reviewed the scope of the closing kills.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — inherits R8.3's lesson.

### R8.3 — DRF-WS, warm-started second backflow pass (Route 8.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Remove DRF's cold-start and narrow-pass confounds: train pass 1 to convergence as the foil, then add a second pass as wide as the first, starting exactly at the identity ($W_4=0$), so its frozen-ratio input is exactly the feature set shown to locate the foil's remaining wrong signs.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R7.2's optimisation sub-component (Round 7 refill, 2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16, Round 8).
* **Kill rule**: pre-registered by the round-7 critic before any code: "KILL if $r\ge0.9$ at 2 or more counted sizes including the largest", with $r=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\mathrm{ws}]/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\mathrm{wide}]$.
* **Evidence**: gap-asserted sizes, both arms from the same trained foil, 300 more SR steps, seeds 11 and 22: at 6×2 $5+5$ ($V=12$) `ws` 0.00858 against `wide` 0.00866 ($r=0.991$); at 4×3 $5+5$ 0.03766 against 0.03801 ($r=0.991$); seed ranges overlap. $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ fell faster for `ws` (0.052 against 0.065 at 6×2). On the same foil the features locate its wrong signs with held-out AUC 0.93 against 0.65 for occupations plus the foil's hidden units.
* **Why this kills it**: with every confound removed, the second pass trains, lowers the total energy, and leaves the sign error equal to width's: the new capacity goes to amplitudes.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: projected training (later tested as R9.2), horizons beyond 300 steps, $V>12$, compiled-cost comparisons. On these toys about 90% of the unprojected sign error is symmetry contamination (projected foil 0.0045 and 0.0017 against 0.050 and 0.011).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: a 300-step, unprojected, $V\le12$ statement (cross-node critic Majors 3 and 4). Accepted as a scope limit; the projected re-check could not be run (NO-TARGET-P), and the projected narrow-foil test R9.2 did not overturn it.
* **Checked by**: cross-node critic (2026-11-16, Majors 3 and 4).
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: Located sign errors are not corrected sign errors: even when features locate where a trained backflow's sign is wrong (AUC 0.93), SR on a feature-fed orbital update spends the new capacity on amplitudes and the sign error moves only as much as width moves it.

### R8.4 — DRF-T, tied re-entry of ratio features into the first pass (Route 8.4) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Avoid buying a second large output layer: the ratio features re-enter pass 1's hidden layer, $h=\tanh(xW_1+b_1)+\tanh([x,f]W_3+b_3)$, with the orbitals still read out by the shared $W_2$, so only about $3VH_1$ parameters are new and the matched foil is barely wider.
* **Lineage**: Operation A on R7.2's parameter-allocation sub-component (round-7 critic refill, 2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16, tie-break).
* **Kill rule**: the R8.3 rule, plus the pre-registered tie-break: "Retrain `widet` with SR shift 0.1 ... apply the same rule with $r=\mathrm{dts}/\mathrm{widets}$ ... UNDECIDED again kills 8.4 at T1".
* **Evidence**: with the recipe's shift ($10^{-3}$) the arm never left its start (first step raised $E$ by 0.68 and the best-energy guard returned the start). At shift 0.1 against a recipe-shift foil: $r=1.19$ and 0.895 (UNDECIDED). Against a shift-matched foil: $r=0.985$ at 6×2 and 0.956 at 4×3.
* **Why this kills it**: once the optimiser setting is matched, the 4×3 win disappears ($r$ rises from 0.895 to 0.956), so it was the shift, not the re-entry.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: re-entry that adds orbital directions outside $W_2$'s span (the round-7 critic's sharpest gap), tested in a different form as R9.0.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: re-entry through a shared output layer cannot add new directions by construction, so this tested a weak member of the family. Raised by the round-7 critic as the route's sharpest gap; answered by R9.0, which does add directions and also tied.
* **Checked by**: cross-node critic (2026-11-16, Majors 3 and 4); the tie-break was run by the orchestrator.
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: Located sign errors are not corrected sign errors: even when features locate where a trained backflow's sign is wrong (AUC 0.93), SR on a feature-fed orbital update spends the new capacity on amplitudes and the sign error moves only as much as width moves it.

### R8.5 — Threshold-scanned exact vote (unnumbered in the Round 7 refill) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Apply the exact vote with one scalar gate whose threshold is chosen by an energy scan, so only confident votes flip signs and the false alarms that made the raw vote harmful are suppressed without training any flip.
* **Lineage**: rejected at T0 during the Round 7 refill (2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is it a known method?
* **Evidence**: a sign set by one exact local application of $H$ with a scalar threshold is R6.8's one-step Lanczos sign with one extra parameter.
* **Why this kills it**: known, and not size-consistent.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: learned, configuration-dependent gates inside a product.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: none raised; not raised by a critic.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — R6.8 relabel.

### R8.6 — Matrix-product-state sign factor along a snake path (unnumbered in the Round 7 refill) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Multiply the foil by a sign factor computed by a matrix product state along a snake path through the lattice, a well-understood, trainable, non-local sign correlator whose cost is set by its bond dimension.
* **Lineage**: rejected at T0 during the Round 7 refill (2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G3 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 3: does it work only by removing the Hard Core (2D)?
* **Evidence**: a snake-path matrix product state has bounded entanglement across cuts; on a 2D lattice the bond dimension needed grows exponentially with the width (area law).
* **Why this kills it**: it is efficient only in quasi-1D, which the Hard Core excludes.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: 2D tensor-network sign factors (PEPS), at much higher cost.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: a sign factor may need far less entanglement than the full state. Not raised by a critic; unresolved.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a known area-law limit.

### R8.7 — Hash table of learned sign flips (unnumbered in the Round 7 refill) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Store learned sign flips for individual configurations in a hash table attached to the foil, so the flips that an unrestricted phase finds can be kept without any parametric model having to express them.
* **Lineage**: rejected at T0 during the Round 7 refill (2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G4 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 4: does the gain survive growing $V$?
* **Evidence**: the table memorizes configurations, so its size grows with the number of configurations visited, exponential in $V$, and it does not generalise to unseen configurations.
* **Why this kills it**: not extensive and not polynomial.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: a parametric carrier whose function class contains the same flips (the restated missing ingredient).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: none raised; not raised by a critic.
* **Checked by**: not reviewed.
* **Lesson**: route-specific — memorization.

### R9.0 — LRK, weight-shared low-rank orbital update fed ratio features (closed-leaf falsifier) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Build the Saturation Diagnostic's named missing structure: a weight-shared per-site kernel (8 hidden units) reads occupations and all-edge ratio features and outputs $V\times2$ factors $U_s,T_s$, so the orbitals become $O_2=O_1+U_s(T_s^{\mathsf T}O_1)$. It adds configuration-dependent new orbital directions with 120 parameters at every $V$, below one width unit (145).
* **Lineage**: reopening-ledger ingredient I1, the falsifier of the Saturation Diagnostic (closed-leaf falsifier, 2026-11-16); its projected narrow-foil regime is R9.2.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: the diagnostic's falsifier under the t1c rule, written before the run: "KILL if $r\ge0.9$ at 2 or more counted sizes including the largest"; only a PASS-W ($r\le0.7$ at every counted size) plus an attribution PASS falsifies the diagnostic.
* **Evidence**: same warm start, 300 more SR steps, seeds 11 and 22, against the foil widened to 1255 parameters: $r=1.05$ at 6×2 (0.00892 against 0.00850) and 1.00 at 4×3 (0.04269 against 0.04267); $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ lower for LRK (0.120 and 0.123 against 0.134 and 0.137 at 4×3).
* **Why this kills it**: the carrier the diagnostic said was missing ties width on sign while beating it on total energy, the R8.3 pattern again.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: carriers fed multi-hop features; projected training (R9.2); $V>12$.
* **Strongest case against the kill**: unprojected toys whose sign error is mostly symmetry contamination. Answered in part by R9.2.
* **Checked by**: critic sign-off of the Leaf Status Summary (2026-11-16), which checked the round 6 to 9 numbers.
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: Located sign errors are not corrected sign errors: even when features locate where a trained backflow's sign is wrong (AUC 0.93), SR on a feature-fed orbital update spends the new capacity on amplitudes and the sign error moves only as much as width moves it.

### R9.1 — RPC, ratio-fed phase carrier on the frozen foil (Route 9.1) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Escape the zero crossing entirely: $\psi=\psi_F(\mathbf n)e^{i\varphi(\mathbf n)}$ with the trained foil $\psi_F$ frozen and $\varphi=\pi\sum_v[\tanh(\phi_vW_k+b_k)\cdot a+c]$ from a weight-shared kernel (57 parameters) on per-site occupations and ratio features; a sign changes by rotation, and the energy couples real and imaginary parts through the shared amplitude.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.1 (real frame to phase frame), from reopening ingredients I3 and U3 (Reopening review, 2026-11-16); translation repair of R6.4 (one shared amplitude instead of two independent parts).
* **Source analogy**: Signal-processing phase retrieval relaxes a lost discrete phase to a continuous variable (Candès, Strohmer & Voroninski, arXiv:1109.4499). Correspondence: lost phase ↔ sign of $\psi$; known magnitudes ↔ frozen foil amplitudes. It works there because random measurements make the relaxed problem well posed; the gap is that here the objective is a fixed local Hamiltonian, so whether a descending phase direction lies in the carrier's span is not guaranteed.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at T1 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: the t1c rule against the matched foil `wid`, written before the run; plus, after the RPC kill and before its own run, the phase-family premise: FAMILY-KILL if the curvature $\lambda(S)=\min_{g\in S}q(g)/\mathrm{var}_{\psi^2}(g)$ is positive for the candidate spans at the counted sizes, where $q(g)=-\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{\mathbf n\mathbf m}\psi_{\mathbf n}\psi_{\mathbf m}H_{\mathbf n\mathbf m}(g_{\mathbf n}-g_{\mathbf m})^2$ is the second-order energy change.
* **Evidence**: in every run the kernel returned to $\lvert\cos\varphi\rvert\ge0.996$ with zero flipped weight, so RPC equals the foil ($r=1.30$ at 6×2, 1.16 at 4×3). An unrestricted per-configuration phase did descend (flipping 1.7% of the weight at 4×3, $r_{\mathrm{free}}=0.77$, but 1.17 at 6×2). Curvature: $\lambda$ for linear ratio spans 0.35 and 0.31, quadratic 0.26 and 0.22, occupations 0.37 and 0.33, against $-1.76$ and $-1.70$ for the unrestricted direction (6×2, 4×3).
* **Why this kills it**: the foil is a saddle in phase space, but every extensive feature span tried has positive curvature, so no carrier on these features can leave $\varphi=0$; the obstruction is the non-locality of the needed correction in feature space, not the zero crossing.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: spans with $\lambda(S)<0$, for example multi-hop features coupling ratios across neighbouring edges (the restated missing ingredient).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: only per-site linear and quadratic spans were screened. Not raised by a critic; accepted as the scope of the kill, and it defines the leaf's open ledger row.
* **Checked by**: critic sign-off of the Leaf Status Summary (2026-11-16).
* **Lesson**: LESSON?: A phase carrier escapes the zero-crossing barrier but not the span barrier: at a real foil the energy gradient in phase is zero, so SR moves only if the carrier's span contains a negative-curvature direction, which the second-order screen $\lambda(S)$ tests before any training.

### R9.2 — LRK-PN, the low-rank carrier on a narrow projected foil (Route 9.2) (SHELVED)
* **The idea**: Test the diagnostic in the projected regime it was scoped away from: LRK unchanged on a deliberately narrow projected foil (NNBF width 2, 530 parameters), which restores a sign-error target on ED toys at a capacity-to-difficulty ratio closer to the 4×4 instance's.
* **Lineage**: Operation A from reopening item U1 (projected training with a target) (Reopening review, 2026-11-16).
* **Outcome**: SHELVED (UNDECIDED at T1 under RULE N; the next step was not run because it cannot reach PASS-W).
* **Why shelved**: at equal steps LRK beat width at 6×2 ($r=0.56$; 0.0028 against 0.0050) but tied at 4×3 ($r=1.02$); its 6×2 win came with an equal fall in $\Delta E_{\mathrm{tot}}$ and shrank to 10% at equal CPU time ($q=0.90$ against a width arm run for 1150 steps). Since 4×3 already ties at equal steps, no seed extension or compiled re-timing can give $r\le0.7$ at every counted size; the predicted verdict of any further step is "not PASS-W", which cannot change the decision (compute-gate G2 reasoning). The ledger had used 1488 of 1800 CPU-s.
* **What would unshelve it**: a compiled re-timing that puts LRK's step cost below about twice width's together with a third gap-asserted projected toy where LRK wins at equal CPU time, or a carrier passing the curvature screen that can be run in this regime.

### R9.3 — Fixed-node objective for the nodes, self-healing DMC on the lattice (Route 9.3) (KILLED)
* **The idea**: Optimise the network's nodes directly against a fixed-node objective (the energy of the best state with those signs), as self-healing diffusion Monte Carlo does in the continuum, so node updates follow the quantity that matters rather than the variational energy.
* **Lineage**: Operation B.3, cross-field translation from self-healing DMC, built from reopening ingredient I3 (Reopening review, 2026-11-16).
* **Source analogy**: In continuum quantum Monte Carlo, self-healing DMC (Reboredo, Hood & Kent, arXiv:0808.3417) iteratively improves nodes from the fixed-node projected walker distribution. Correspondence: nodal surface ↔ sign of the network; fixed-node DMC ↔ lattice fixed-node Green's-function Monte Carlo. It works there as a systematic improvement in a large basis; the gap is that a 2026 assessment (arXiv:2609.01301) finds it converges to a wrong node in any finite basis, and the lattice form needs FN-GFMC with the network as guide.
* **Outcome**: KILLED at G0 (2026-11-16).
* **Kill rule**: Gate 0: is it known, and does it fix the known adoption barrier?
* **Evidence**: self-healing DMC is published, its finite-basis failure is documented, and its lattice form is the P1.1 Gate 0 kill (fixed-node GFMC with an NQS guide; Loehr & Clark, arXiv:2510.26906).
* **Why this kills it**: known, with a documented convergence failure, and no new mechanism.
* **What the kill does not rule out**: using $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$-like fixed-sign energies as diagnostics (as this leaf does).
* **Strongest case against the kill**: the lattice may behave differently from continuum finite bases. Not raised by a critic; unresolved.
* **Checked by**: critic sign-off of the Leaf Status Summary (2026-11-16).
* **Lesson**: route-specific — a known method with a documented failure.

### R9.4 — Converged-horizon or equal-wall-time LRK (Route 9.4) (SHELVED)
* **The idea**: Answer the cross-node critic's 300-step objection by running LRK against width to convergence, or at equal wall time instead of equal parameters, to see whether the tie is a learning-speed artefact rather than a capacity statement.
* **Lineage**: Operation A from reopening item U2 (Reopening review, 2026-11-16); the leaf recorded it as "KILLED at T0 under the value-of-information rule", which this card re-labels SHELVED because nothing was refuted.
* **Outcome**: SHELVED (G2 predicted outcome; refused before any run).
* **Why shelved**: past 300 steps at ED size the width arm moves toward fitting the whole symmetry sector, and the equal-CPU arm of R9.2 (`widNL`) already showed that extra steps give width most of LRK's gain; a tie was predicted with probability at least 0.8, and a tie leaves the decision unchanged. Capped cost: a few CPU-minutes per size at T1.
* **What would unshelve it**: a compiled re-timing that puts LRK's step cost below twice width's step cost, which would make the equal-wall-time comparison favour LRK.

## Leaf Status Summary
* **State**: DORMANT
* **Updated**: 2026-11-17, documentation pass (Analogy Map, Route Register, reopening ledger); no new result. Last result: 2026-11-16, the Reopening review (route RPC killed at T1, route LRK-PN UNDECIDED at T1 and shelved, recommendation STAY-CLOSED).
* **Conclusion**: A trained neural backflow's own determinant ratios tell where its remaining wrong signs are (held-out AUC 0.93 at 12 sites), but no tested sign layer turned that knowledge into a lower sign error than the same parameters spent on backflow width: the warm-started layers tied ($r$ between 0.96 and 1.05 at the counted sizes), and the two wins were not sign wins (on the $4\times4$ instance FRD-K's 16% gain was amplitude-borne; on a narrow projected 6×2 toy LRK's per-step win shrank to 10% at equal CPU time and came with a similar fall in total energy). The obstruction found last is not the zero-crossing barrier: the descent directions that would fix the signs exist but lie outside every extensive feature span tried. The leaf is not EXHAUSTED, because the restated missing ingredient and the larger-size regime have no killed route yet.
* **Scope**: exact-sum training and scoring on 2-leg and 3-leg $t$–$t'$–$J$ toys with $V\le12$ sites ($t'=-0.25$, $J=0.4$, two holes, gap $E_1-E_0\ge0.02t$ on counted sizes), mostly unprojected, 300 warm-started SR steps, two seeds, matched parameter count; one projected narrow-foil regime (R9.2); the FRD family also on the $4\times4$ open $6+6$ instance with space-group projection. Sign half only; no competing-order claim.
* **Compute spent**: 4,401 CPU-h on Slurm (master Compute Ledger: 410216, 451916, 553724, 697584, 698178; 451916 alone is 3,260), plus about 2.2 CPU-h of single-thread login-node T1 runs (rounds 6 to 9). Rounds 2 and 3 left no job ids.
* **Entered because**: a Saturation Diagnostic was adopted (section 3b, first trigger): the Round 7 critic's conditional diagnostic took effect on 2026-11-16 once R8.4 was killed by its tie-break. The critic sign-off then attempted a closure certificate, and its item 5 ("missing ingredients exhausted") could not be filled honestly (third trigger), because ledger rows 4 and 9 below have no route.

**The question.** A neural quantum state (NQS) writes the wave function $\psi(\mathbf n)$ on occupation configurations $\mathbf n$ of $V$ lattice sites; for doped, frustrated fermions its sign (the nodes) is the hard part. The standard fermionic NQS, neural-network backflow (NNBF), is a Slater determinant whose orbitals a small network computes from $\mathbf n$, so its nodes move only by deforming orbitals from a mean-field anchor. P1.2 asks whether an added sign-changing layer, at about one determinant's cost, reaches better nodes. The score is $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$, an exact upper bound on the energy error of the best non-negative amplitudes under the ansatz's sign, compared with a matched-width foil (an NNBF made wider to the same parameter count, trained by the same stochastic reconfiguration (SR) recipe) through $r=\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\text{route}]/\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}[\text{foil}]$.

**The mechanism tried.** The ground state obeys $\psi_0(\mathbf n)(E_0-H_{\mathbf n\mathbf n})=\sum_{\mathbf m}H_{\mathbf n\mathbf m}\psi_0(\mathbf m)$, so the true sign at $\mathbf n$ is a weighted vote of its Hamiltonian neighbours. The determinant supplies it through ratios $R_e=c_e\Phi(\mathbf m_e)/\Phi(\mathbf n)$ over edges $e$ (hops or exchanges, $c_e$ the signed matrix element); "frozen" ratios are Green's-function entries at one inverse per amplitude. The routes fed it through different carriers: flips, positive factors, a second backflow pass, a low-rank orbital update, a phase.

**The chain of evidence.**
1. Vote factors $\Phi\prod_e[1+\alpha_eg(R_e)]$ on an NNBF base (FRD-K, R5.1), on the projected $4\times4$ instance (1,681,680 states), beat width 6 at matched parameters (1719 against 1734) with $r=0.84$ over 3 seeds (451916). Removing every flip moved $\Delta E_{\mathrm{sign}}$ from 0.009751 to 0.009757 (697584): the gain was amplitude, not vote. Its cost grows as $(1+E_{\mathrm{act}})$ NNBF passes per local energy, with $E_{\mathrm{act}}\propto V$ active edges.
2. Positive factors on exact ratios (R6.3, R7.1) beat a neural Jastrow but lost to width ($r$ 2.04, 0.95, 1.19 at $V=8,10,12$).
3. A second backflow pass fed frozen ratios lost cold-started (R7.2 to R7.4, $r$ 1.4 to 2.3). Yet on the trained foil those features locate its wrong signs (AUC 0.93 against 0.65 for occupations plus hidden units). Warm-started, as wide as pass 1, on gapped sizes, it lowered the total energy faster (0.052 against 0.065 at 6×2) but tied on sign ($r=0.991$ at 6×2 and 4×3; R8.3); tied re-entry tied once the optimiser shift was matched ($r$ 0.985, 0.956; R8.4).
4. The diagnostic's own falsifier, a weight-shared low-rank orbital update with 120 parameters (below one width unit, 145), tied again ($r$ 1.05, 1.00; R9.0).
5. A phase carrier $e^{i\varphi(\mathbf n)}$ with no zero crossing returned to $\varphi\approx0$ ($r$ 1.30, 1.16; R9.1): its second-order curvature is positive (0.22 to 0.35) for every per-site linear or quadratic span of the ratio features, while an unrestricted per-configuration direction descends ($\approx-1.7$).

Fairness: same start, matched parameters, same SR recipe and steps, exact sums, rules written before each run.

**Why the alternatives fail.** Discrete flips are left unused by SR (R5.1, R6.7). The exact projected vote helped once (removing 1.33 times FRD-K's sign advantage on one state) but is a one-step Lanczos sign (R6.8, R8.1) costing $(1+E_{\mathrm{act}})\lvert G\rvert$ passes per amplitude (R6.7). A fixed vote on frozen ratios raises the sign error 1.3 to 5.2 times (R6.7F, R5.4). Local-occupation layers cannot see the nodes (R4, R2.1); a complex detour decouples (R6.4); an unrestricted phase beats width only at 4×3 ($r=0.77$), with one parameter per configuration.

**What this leaf taught beyond itself** (provisional tags; the orchestrator numbers them):
* Local-occupation sign layers cannot locate a reference's nodes (R4, R2.1).
* Linear dressings are not size-consistent (R2.3).
* Credit a sign-layer gain only after a flips-off read-out (R5.1).
* Check that a pending job's rule can still pass (R5.1).
* An informative vote is not a trainable vote (R5.2).
* Positive correlators lose to width on sign (R6.3, R7.1).
* Exact neighbour features hide an $\mathcal O(V)$ cost (R6.7).
* Fixed votes on frozen ratios harm; learned features help (R6.7F).
* Premises on the start do not predict trained gains (R7.2, R7.4).
* Counted toy sizes need a gap (R7.3).
* Located sign errors are not corrected sign errors (R8.3, R8.4, R9.0).
* Screen phase spans by curvature $\lambda(S)$ first (R9.1).

**Strongest objection and answer.** "These are 300-step runs on unprojected 12-site toys, where projection removes about 90% of the sign error." Partly answered: on a narrow projected foil LRK ties at 4×3 and its 6×2 win shrinks to 10% at equal CPU time (R9.2); P1.3 finds the projected $4\times4$ sign error capacity-limited. Unanswered for continuous carriers on the $4\times4$ instance.

**Limits.** Not covered: $V>12$, other $t'$ or $J$, sampled-force training, long horizons, compiled cost constants.

**Most promising direction.** A carrier whose function class contains the configuration-specific phase-descent directions with a parameter count independent of $V$, for example message-passing features that couple ratios across neighbouring edges (Analogy Map, B3). For: the direction exists ($\lambda\approx-1.7$). Against: every extensive span tried has $\lambda>0$ (ratio features 0.22 against occupations 0.33 at 4×3).

**Next step.** No job in flight. T1: $\lambda(S)$ via `t1f_curv.py` for each candidate span at 6×2 and 4×3 (under 10 CPU-minutes). A span with $\lambda(S)<0$ at both sizes is trained against `wid` under the t1c rule (KILL if $r\ge0.9$ at 2 counted sizes including the largest; PASS-W if $r\le0.7$ at every counted size), then the attribution control.

**What would close it.** EXHAUSTED: ledger rows 4 and 9 closed by a kill or a gate refusal. ANSWERED: a carrier reaches PASS-W with attribution, then beats projected width-6 NNBF on the $4\times4$ instance at matched wall time.
